T_{Q}N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}(Q)_{|Q}=\mathcal{O}(Q)_{|\alpha(P)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\alpha^{*}\mathcal{O}(Q)_{|P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}(eP+R)_{|P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}(P)_{|P}^{\otimes e}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{P}M^{\otimes e}.

If
z
(resp.
w
) is a uniformizing parameter on
M
at
P
(resp. on
N
at
Q
) such that
\alpha^{*}(w)=z^{e}
, then:

\iota_{1}\left((\partial/\partial w)_{\mid Q}\right)=(\partial/\partial z)_{\mid P}^{\otimes e}.

We may also consider the complex line
\langle\mathcal{O}(Q),\mathcal{O}(Q)\rangle
, defined as the Deligne pairing with itself of the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(Q)
on the projective curve
N
. By the very definition of the Deligne pairing, we have a canonical isomorphism:

\iota_{2}:\langle\mathcal{O}(Q),\mathcal{O}(Q)\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}(Q)_{|Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{Q}N.

Since
g
is a Green function for
P
on
M
, we may equip the line
T_{P}M
with the capacitary metric
\|.\|_{g}^{\mathrm{cap}}
, and the line
T_{P}M^{\otimes e}
with its
e
-th tensor power
\|.\|_{g}^{\mathrm{cap},\otimes e}
.

Since
{\mathrm{supp}\,}g
is compact, we may consider the Green function
\alpha_{*}g
for
Q
in
N
, and equip
\mathcal{O}(Q)
with the associated Hermitian metric
\|.\|_{\alpha_{*}g}
, defined by the equality:

\|\mathbbm{1}_{Q}(y)\|_{\alpha_{*}g}=e^{-\alpha_{*}g(y)}=\exp\left(-\int_{M}g\delta_{\alpha^{*}(y)}\right)

for every
y\in N\setminus\{Q\}
. In turn, to the Hermitian metric
\|.\|_{\alpha_{*}g}
on the line bundle
{\mathcal{O}}(Q)
, is associated a Hermitian metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\alpha_{*}g}
on the complex line
\langle\mathcal{O}(Q),\mathcal{O}(Q)\rangle
by the construction in
[
Del87
]
, which immediately extends to the present setting, where the Hermitian metric on line bundles over complex projective curves are not necessarily
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
but have
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
regularity.

The following proposition describes the compatibility between the isomorphism of complex lines:

\iota_{1}\circ\iota_{2}:\langle\mathcal{O}(Q),\mathcal{O}(Q)\rangle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{P}M^{\otimes e}

and the Hermitian norms
\|.\|^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\alpha_{*}g}
and
\|.\|_{g}^{\mathrm{cap},\otimes e}
on these lines.

Proposition 5.1.2
.

For any
v\in\langle\mathcal{O}(Q),\mathcal{O}(Q)\rangle
, the following equality holds:

(5.1.3)

\|\iota_{1}\circ\iota_{2}(v)\|_{g}^{\mathrm{cap},\otimes e}=\exp\left({-\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha,g)}\right)\|v\|^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\alpha_{*}g}.

The equality (
5.1.3
) is a straightforward consequence of the definitions of the Hermitian norms

\|.\|_{g}^{\mathrm{cap}}
and
\|.\|^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\alpha_{*}g}
. Proposition
5.1.2
provides a motivation for the introduction of the invariant
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha,g)
, and explains why it arises in the computations of arithmetic intersection numbers in the following sections. However we will not use Proposition
5.1.2
explicitly in the sequel, and the details of its proof will be left to the reader.

5.2.
The invariant
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)

In this section, we consider a connected compact Riemann surface with boundary
V
with non-empty boundary
\partial V
, and
P
a point in its interior
\mathring{V}
.

We denote by
g_{V,P}
be the Green function associated to the point
P
of the Riemann surface with boundary
V
and by
\mu_{V,P}
its harmonic measure, as defined in
4.3.1
above.

Definition 5.2.1
.

For every non-constant complex analytic map:

\alpha:V^{+}\longrightarrow N

from
V^{+}
to a Riemann surface
N
, we define the
overflow
of the morphism
\alpha
from the pointed compact Riemann surface with boundary
(V,P)
to
N
as the real number:

(5.2.1)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N):=\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha,g_{V,P}).

If we denote by
e
the ramification index of
\alpha
at the point
P
, according to (
5.1.2
), we have:

(5.2.2)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)=\int_{V}g_{V,P}\left(\delta_{\alpha^{*}(\alpha(P))-eP}+\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\mu_{V,P}\right).

The expression (
5.2.2
) shows that, like the Green function
g_{V,P}
and the measure
\mu_{V,P}
, the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
is nonnegative.

Using that the measure:

g_{V,P}\,\omega(g_{V,P})=g_{V,P}\,\mu_{V,P}

is zero, one readily checks that
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
also admits the following expression in terms of
\ast
-product:

(5.2.3)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)=\int_{V}(\alpha^{\ast}\alpha_{\ast}g_{V,P}-e\,g_{V,P})\ast g_{V,P}.

Recall that a morphism of complex analytic spaces is called
finite
when it is proper with finite fibers. Finite morphisms between Riemann surfaces are classically known as “ramified coverings.”

Proposition 5.2.2
.

With the notation of Definition
5.2.1
, the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
vanishes if and only if the complex analytic map

\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}:\mathring{V}\rightarrow\alpha(\mathring{V})

is a finite morphism totally ramified
1
1

1

namely, a finite analytic morphism of degree the ramification index
e
of
\alpha
at
P
.
at
P
.

In particular, when
e=1
, that is when
\alpha
is étale at
P
, the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
vanishes if and only if
\alpha_{\mathring{V}}
is an open embedding of Riemann surfaces.

The non-negativity of the invariant
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
and the above characterization of its vanishing when
e=1
motivates the terminology
overflow
(or
excess
) for this invariant.
The notation
\mathrm{Ex}
stands for
ex
cess or
ex
undatio.

The proof of Proposition
5.2.2
will rely on the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2.3
.

The complex analytic map
\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}:\mathring{V}\rightarrow\alpha(\mathring{V})
is a finite morphism if and only if the following equality holds:

(5.2.4)

\alpha(\partial V)\cap\alpha(\mathring{V})=\emptyset.

Proof.

Since
\alpha
is nonconstant and
V
is compact, the fibers of
\alpha_{\mid V}
are finite. Therefore
\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}:\mathring{V}\rightarrow\alpha(\mathring{V})
is a finite morphism if and only if it is a proper holomorphic map, namely if and only if, for any compact subset
K
of
\alpha(\mathring{V})
,
\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap\mathring{V}
is compact.

The equality (
5.2.4
) is readily seen to be equivalent to:

(5.2.5)

\alpha^{-1}(\alpha(\mathring{V}))\cap\partial V=\emptyset.

Assume that (
5.2.4
) holds, and let
K
be a compact subset of
N
contained in
\alpha(\mathring{V})
. Then
\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap V
is closed in the compact manifold with boundary
V
, so that it is compact. The equality (
5.2.5
) implies:

\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap\partial V=\emptyset

so that
\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap V
is actually contained in
\mathring{V}
. This proves that
\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap\mathring{V}
is compact.

Conversely, assume that the intersection
\alpha(\partial V)\cap\alpha(\mathring{V})
is nonempty, and let
x
(resp.
y
) be a point of
\mathring{V}
(resp.
\partial V
) such that
\alpha(x)=\alpha(y)
. Since
\alpha(\partial V)
is nowhere dense in
N
, we may find a compact subset
K
of the open set
\alpha(\mathring{V})
of
N
such that
K
contains
\alpha(x)
and
K\setminus\alpha(\partial V)
is dense in
K
; for instance, a small disk containing
\alpha(x)
in its interior will do.

Since
K\setminus\alpha(\partial V)
is dense in
K
and the map
\alpha
is open, the set
\alpha^{-1}(K)\setminus\partial V
is dense in
\alpha^{-1}(K).
In particular,

\alpha^{-1}(K)\cap\mathring{V}=\alpha^{-1}(K)\setminus\partial V

is not compact, as its closure in
V
contains
y\in\partial V.

∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2.2
.

If we denote by
e
the ramification index of
\alpha
at
P
, we may write:

(5.2.6)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)=\int_{V}g_{V,P}\,\delta_{\alpha^{*}(\alpha(P))-eP}+\int_{N}\alpha_{*}g_{V,P}\,\alpha_{*}\mu_{V,P}.

The function
g_{V,P}
is positive on
\mathring{V}
and vanishes on
V^{+}\setminus\mathring{V}
. Consequently the function
\alpha_{*}g_{V,P}
is positive on
\alpha(\mathring{V})
, and vanishes on
N\setminus\alpha(\mathring{V})
. Moreover the support of the positive measure
\mu_{V,P}
is
\partial V
, and therefore the support of the positive measure
\alpha_{*}\mu_{V,P}
is
\alpha(\partial V).

This implies that the integral:

\int_{V}g_{V,P}\,\delta_{\alpha^{*}(\alpha(P))-eP}

vanishes if and only if the support of the divisor
\alpha^{*}(\alpha(P))-eP
is disjoint of
{\mathring{V}}
, and that
the integral:

\int_{N}\alpha_{*}g_{V,P}\,\alpha_{*}\mu_{V,P}

vanishes if and only if the intersection:

\alpha(\partial V)\cap\alpha(\mathring{V})

is empty, and therefore, according to Lemma
5.2.3
, if and only if
\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}:\mathring{V}\rightarrow\alpha(\mathring{V})
is a finite morphism.

Finally the expression (
5.2.6
) for the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
shows that it vanishes if and only if
\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}:\mathring{V}\rightarrow\alpha(\mathring{V})
is a finite morphism and:

\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}^{\ast}(\alpha(P))=eP.

This last condition precisely means that the finite morphism
\alpha_{|\mathring{V}}
is totally ramified at
P
.
∎

5.3.
Green functions for the diagonal of a Riemann surface

In addition to its definition by formulas (
5.2.2
) or (
5.2.6
), the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
admits an alternative expression that will be established in
the next section, and turns out to be useful in applications, as demonstrated by its role in the derivation of the “arithmetic holononomicity theorem” of
[
CDT21
]
in Section
8.3.2
.

Besides the Green function
g_{V,P}
and the harmonic measure
\mu_{V,P}
, this alternative expression involves a suitable notion of Green function for the diagonal of a Riemann surface
N
, possibly non-compact, which we introduce in this section.

5.3.1.

Let
N
be a Riemann surface equipped with a real
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}

2
-form
\beta
.

We define
a Green function for the diagonal of
N
associated to the
2
-form
\beta

as a function:

g:N\times N\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty]

that satisfies the following three conditions:

(1)

The function
g
is symmetric;
namely, for every
(Q_{1},Q_{2})\in N^{2}
, we have:

g(Q_{1},Q_{2})=g(Q_{2},Q_{1}).

(2)

On the complement
N\times N\setminus\Delta_{N}
of the diagonal
\Delta_{N}
of
N
,
g_{N}
takes values in
\mathbb{R}
and is
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
. Along
\Delta_{N}
,
g
admits a logarithmic singularity
; namely, for every local complex analytic coordinate
z
defined on some open subset
U
of
N
, there exists a function
h
in
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(U\times U,\mathbb{R})
such that, for every
(Q_{1},Q_{2})
in
U^{2}
:

(5.3.1)

g(Q_{1},Q_{2})=\log|z(Q_{1}-z(Q_{2})|^{-1}+h(Q_{1},Q_{2}).

This implies that
g
is locally
L^{1}
on
N\times N
and defines a distribution on
N\times N
such that the current:

\omega(g):=\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g+\delta_{\Delta_{N}}

is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}

2
-form on
N\times N.

(3)

If
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
denote the two projections from
N\times N
onto
N
, then the
2
-form
\omega(g)-p_{1}^{*}\beta-p_{2}^{*}\beta
vanishes on the fibers of
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
.

When these conditions are satisfied, then for every point
Q
of
N
, the function
g(Q,.)
is a Green function with
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
regularity for the divisor
Q
in
N
, and we have:

\omega(g(Q,.))=\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g(Q,.)+\delta_{Q}=(Q,\mathrm{Id}_{N})^{*}\omega(g)=\beta.

The capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g(Q,.)}
on
T_{Q}N
will be denoted by
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g}.
With the notation of (
5.3.1
), we have:

\|(\partial/\partial z)_{Q}\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g}=e^{-h(Q,Q)}

for every
Q
in
U
. In particular, this construction defines a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g}
on the tangent bundle
T_{N}
of
N
. Alternatively, this metric may be defined as follows: the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(\Delta_{N})
on
N\times N
may be endowed with the
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
metric
\|.\|_{g}
defined by the equality:

\|\mathbbm{1}_{\Delta_{N}}\|_{g}=e^{-g},

and the adjunction isomorphism:

\mathcal{O}(\Delta_{N})_{|\Delta_{N}}\simeq N_{\Delta_{N}}(N\times N)\simeq T_{N}

becomes an isometry when
\mathcal{O}(\Delta_{N})_{|\Delta_{N}}
is endowed with the restriction of the metric
\|.\|_{g}
and
T_{N}
with the capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g}.

5.3.2.
Examples

The function:

g_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbb{C}^{2}\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty],\,\,(z,z^{\prime})\mapsto\log|z-z^{\prime}|^{-1}

is a Green function for the diagonal of
\mathbb{C}
associated to the
2
-form
\beta=0.
The capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\mathbb{C}}}
on
T_{\mathbb{C}}
is the “standard” metric, which satisfies:

\|\partial/\partial z\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\mathbb{C}}}=1.

The function:

g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}:\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})^{2}\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty]

defined by:

(5.3.2)

g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}((z_{0}:z_{1}),(z^{\prime}_{0}:z^{\prime}_{1})):=\log\bigg|\frac{z_{0}z^{\prime}_{1}-z_{1}z^{\prime}_{0}}{(|z_{0}|^{2}+|z_{1}|^{2})^{1/2}(|z^{\prime}_{0}|^{2}+|z^{\prime}_{1}|^{2})^{1/2}}\bigg|^{-1}

is a Green function for the diagonal in
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
associated to the Fubini-Study 2-form
\beta=\omega_{\rm{FS}}
, defined by:

\omega_{\rm{FS}\mid\mathbb{C}}:=\frac{i}{2\pi}\frac{dz\wedge d\overline{z}}{(1+|z|^{2})^{2}}.

The Green function
g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
is nonnegative everywhere, and it is invariant under the diagonal action of
U(2)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})^{2}
.
The capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}}
on
T_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
is the
U(2)
-invariant metric such that:

\|(\partial/\partial z)_{\mid 0}\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}}=1.

When the Riemann surface
N
is compact and connected, and when the real
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}

2
-form
\beta
satisfies:

(5.3.3)

\int_{N}\beta=1,

then there exists a Green function
g
for the diagonal of
N
associated to
\beta
in the sense above. It is unique up to an additive constant.

Indeed, if
(\alpha_{k})_{1\leq k\leq q}
is an orthogonal basis of
\Omega^{1}(N)
endowed with its canonical Hermitian scalar product
\langle.,.\rangle
defined by:

\langle\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\rangle:=\frac{i}{2}\int_{N}\alpha\wedge\overline{\alpha}^{\prime},

then the closed real
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}

2
-form on
N\times N
:

\gamma:=p_{1}^{*}\beta+p_{2}^{*}\beta+\frac{i}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{q}(p_{1}^{*}\alpha_{k}\wedge p_{2}^{*}\overline{\alpha}_{k}-p_{1}^{*}\overline{\alpha}_{k}\wedge p_{2}^{*}\alpha_{k})

defines the same cohomology class as the current
\delta_{\Delta_{N}}
. Therefore, according to the
\partial\overline{\partial}
-lemma, which applies on the compact Kähler manifold
N\times N,
there exists a real distribution
g
on
N\times N
, unique up to some additive constant, such that:

\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g=\gamma-\delta_{\Delta_{N}}.

Using the ellipticity properties of the operator
\partial\overline{\partial},
one easily see that the distribution
g
is defined by a Green function for the diagonal of
N
associated to
\beta
.
2
2

2

A similar but more elementary argument shows that, conversely, the normalization condition (
5.3.3
) is implied by the existence of a Green function for the diagonal of
N
associated to
\beta
.

Moreover the integral:

\int_{N}g(P,.)\beta

is independent of
P
in
N
. Indeed this integral is easily seen to define a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
function of
P
and the associated distribution on
N
to coincide with

p_{1\ast}(g\,p_{2}^{\ast}\beta).

This distribution is harmonic, and therefore coincides with a constant function, since we have:

\displaystyle i\partial\overline{\partial}\,p_{1\ast}(g\,p_{2}^{\ast}\beta)

\displaystyle=p_{1\ast}(i\partial\overline{\partial}g\wedge p_{2}^{\ast}\beta))

\displaystyle=\pi\,p_{1\ast}\left((\gamma-\delta_{\Delta_{N}})\wedge p^{\ast}_{2}\beta\right)

\displaystyle=\pi\,p_{1\ast}(\gamma\wedge p^{\ast}_{2}\beta)-\pi\beta

\displaystyle=\pi\,p_{1\ast}(p^{\ast}_{1}\beta\wedge p^{\ast}_{2}\beta)-\pi\beta

\displaystyle=\pi\,\left(\int_{N}\beta\right)\,\beta-\pi\beta=0.

This shows that there exists a Green function
g
for the diagonal of
N
associated to
\beta
that moreover satisfies the condition:

\int_{N}g(P,.)\,\beta=0\quad\mbox{for every $Q\in N$.}

This normalized Green function is uniquely determined by
\beta
.

When
\beta
is the normalized volume form associated to some conformal Riemannian metric
\mathbf{g}
on
N
, the normalized Green function
g
coincides with the usual Green function associated to the Laplace operator on the Riemannian manifold
(N,\mathbf{g})
.

When the genus
q
is positive and when
\beta
is the
2
-form:

(5.3.4)

\beta_{\mathrm{Ar}}:=\frac{i}{2q}\sum_{k=1}^{q}\alpha_{k}\wedge\overline{\alpha}_{k},

the normalized Green function
g
is the Arakelov-Green function of
N
, introduced by Arakelov in
[
Ara74
]
.

5.4.
Overflow and Green functions for the diagonal

In this section, we consider a connected compact Riemann surface
V
with nonempty boundary
\partial V
, and
P
a point of its interior
\mathring{V}
. We also consider a Riemann surface
N
(without boundary) endowed with a smooth
2
-form
\beta
, and a Green function
g_{N}
for the diagonal of
N
associated to
\beta
.

5.4.1.
An alternative expression for
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)

To formulate the alternative expression for the overflow that constitutes the object of this section, we need to introduce some notation.

Let
\alpha:V^{+}\rightarrow N
be a non-constant holomorphic map, and let
e
be the ramification index of
\alpha
at
P
. Then the
e
-the jet
\alpha^{[e]}(P)
of
\alpha
at
P
may be identified with a non-zero element of the complex line:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(T_{P}V^{\otimes e},T_{\alpha(P)}N)\simeq T_{P}V^{\vee,\otimes e}\otimes T_{\alpha(P)}N.

If
z
(resp.
w
) is a uniformizing parameter on
M
at
P
(resp. on
N
at
Q
) such that
\alpha^{*}(w)=z^{e}
, then:

\alpha^{[e]}(P)=(dz_{\mid P})^{\otimes e}\otimes(\partial/\partial w)_{\mid Q}.

The complex line
T_{P}V^{\vee,\otimes e}\otimes T_{\alpha(P)}N
may be endowed with the Hermitian norm
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}
deduced by duality and tensor products from the capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{V,P}
on
T_{P}V
and the capacitary metric
\|.\|^{\rm cap}_{g_{N}}
on
T_{\alpha(P)}N
.

Theorem 5.4.1
.

With the previous notation, for any nonconstant holomorphic map

\alpha:V^{+}\rightarrow N
of ramification index
e
at
P
,
the following equality holds:

(5.4.1)

\mathrm{Ex}\big(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)\big)=2\int_{V}g_{V,P}\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{(\partial V)^{2}}g_{N}(\alpha(z_{1}),\alpha(z_{2}))\,d\mu_{V,P}(z_{1})\,d\mu_{V,P}(z_{2})\\
-\log\|\alpha^{[e]}(P)\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}.

The second integral in the right-hand side of (
5.4.1
) is well-defined. Indeed the function:

(\partial V)^{2}\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty],\quad(z_{1},z_{2})\longmapsto g_{N}(\alpha(z_{1}),\alpha(z_{2}))

is integrable with respect to the measure
\mu_{V,P}\otimes\mu_{V,P}
on
(\partial V)^{2}
. Actually, as a consequence of the logarithmic behavior of
g_{N}
along the diagonal, this function is locally
L^{1}
on the compact
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
surface
(\partial V)^{2}
,
3
3

3

This follows for instance from the following elementary result: if
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
are two
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
immersions of
(0,1)
into
\mathbb{C}
and
n_{1}
and
n_{2},
two positive integers, then

\log|\gamma_{1}(t_{1})^{n_{1}}-\gamma_{2}(t_{2})^{n_{2}}|^{-1}
is a locally
L^{1}
function of
(t_{1},t_{2})\in(0,1)^{2}
.
and the measure
\mu_{V,P}\otimes\mu_{V,P}
is defined by a continuous density on
(\partial V)^{2}
. This integrability will also be a consequence of the proof below.

Proof.

For simplicity, we write
g
(resp.
\mu
) for
g_{V,P}
(resp.
\mu_{V,P}
). They satisfy the following equality of currents on
V^{+}:

(5.4.2)

\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g=\mu-\delta_{P}.

Moreover we have:

\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g_{N}=\gamma-\delta_{\Delta_{N}},

where
\gamma
is a smooth
2
-form such that, for any
Q\in N
,

(\mathrm{Id}_{N},Q)^{*}\gamma=\beta.

For every
x
in
V
, we have the following equality of currents on
V^{+}
:

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\delta_{x}=\alpha^{*}\delta_{\alpha(x)}

\displaystyle=(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}\delta_{\Delta_{N}}

\displaystyle=(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}(\gamma-\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g_{N})

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\beta-\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}g_{N}.

Consequently, for every
x
in
V\setminus\alpha^{-1}(\alpha(P)),
we have:

\displaystyle\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\delta_{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{V^{+}}g\,\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}g_{N}

(5.4.3)

\displaystyle=\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{V^{+}}(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}g_{N}\,\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g

(5.4.4)

\displaystyle=\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{V}(\alpha,\alpha(x))^{*}g_{N}\,(\mu-\delta_{P})

\displaystyle=\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{x^{\prime}\in\partial V}g_{N}(\alpha(x^{\prime}),\alpha(x))\,d\mu(x^{\prime})+g_{N}(\alpha(P),\alpha(x)).

The equality (
5.4.3
) follows from Green’s formula, and (
5.4.4
) follows from (
5.4.2
).

The intersection of
\alpha^{-1}(\alpha(P))
with
\mathrm{supp}\,\mu=\partial V
is finite, and therefore:

\mu(\alpha^{-1}(\alpha(P)))=0,

since
\mu
is defined by a continuous density on the smooth curve
\partial V
. As a consequence:

\displaystyle\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\mu

\displaystyle=\int_{\partial V}\Big(\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\delta_{x}\Big)d\mu(x)

(5.4.5)

\displaystyle=\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta-\int_{(\partial V)^{2}}g_{N}(\alpha(x^{\prime}),\alpha(x))\,d\mu(x^{\prime})\,d\mu(x)+\int_{\partial V}g_{N}(\alpha(P),\alpha(x))\,d\mu(x).

Moreover we have:

\displaystyle\delta_{\alpha^{-1}(\alpha(P))-eP}

\displaystyle=\alpha^{*}\alpha_{*}\delta_{P}-e\,\delta_{P}

\displaystyle=(\alpha,\alpha(P))^{*}\delta_{\Delta_{N}}-e\,\delta_{P}

\displaystyle=(\alpha,\alpha(P))^{*}\left(-\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g_{N}+\gamma\right)-e\left(-\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g+\mu\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi+\alpha^{*}\!\beta-e\mu,

where:

\varphi:=eg-(\alpha,\alpha(P))^{*}g_{N}

is defined a priori as a locally
L^{1}
function on
V^{+}
. It is actually continuous on a neighborhood of
P
, and its value at
P
is:

(5.4.6)

\varphi(P)=\log\|\alpha^{[e]}(P)\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}.

Finally
g
vanishes on
V^{+}\setminus{\mathring{V}}
, in particular on
\partial V={\mathrm{supp}\,}\mu
, and therefore:

\displaystyle\int_{V}g\,\delta_{\alpha^{*}(\alpha(P))-eP}

\displaystyle=\int_{V^{+}}g\,\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi+\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\!\beta

\displaystyle=\int_{V^{+}}\varphi\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}g+\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta

\displaystyle=\int_{V^{+}}\varphi\,(\mu-\delta_{P})+\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\beta

(5.4.7)

\displaystyle=-\varphi(P)-\int_{\partial V}(\alpha,\alpha(P))^{*}\!g_{N}\,\mu+\int_{V}g\,\alpha^{*}\!\beta

The equality (
5.4.1
) follows from adding (
5.4.5
) and (
5.4.7
), together with (
5.4.6
).
∎

5.4.2.
Examples

We may specialize Theorem
5.4.1
to the situation where
N
is the Riemann surface
\mathbb{C}
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
, and
g_{N}
is the Green function introduced in
5.3.2
and

5.3.2
.

When
N=\mathbb{C}
, Theorem
5.4.1
takes the following form:

Proposition 5.4.2
.

For any nonconstant holomorphic map

\alpha:V^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
of ramification index
e
at
P
,
the following equality holds:

(5.4.8)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow\mathbb{C})=\int_{(\partial V)^{2}}\log|\alpha(z_{1})-\alpha(z_{2})|\,d\mu_{V,P}(z_{1})\,d\mu_{V,P}(z_{2})-\log\|\alpha^{[e]}(P)\|_{V,P}^{\mathrm{cap},\otimes(-e)}.

In the last term of the right-hand side of (
5.4.8
), we have used that, as the range
N
of
\alpha
is
\mathbb{C},
the
e
-th jet
\alpha^{[e]}(P)
of
\alpha
at
P
is an element of the line:

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{C}}(T_{P}V^{\otimes e},T_{\alpha(P)}\mathbb{C})\simeq(T_{P}V^{\vee})^{\otimes e},

and that the Hermitian norm
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{V,P}
on this line is the norm
\|.\|_{V,P}^{\mathrm{cap},\otimes(-e)}
on
(T_{P}V^{\vee})^{\otimes e}
deduced from the capacitary norm
\|.\|_{V,P}^{\mathrm{cap}}
on
T_{P}V
by duality and tensor power. In terms of some local analytic coordinate
z
on
M
defined on an open neighborhood of
P,
we have:

\alpha^{[e]}(P)=\frac{1}{e!}\frac{d^{e}\alpha(P)}{dz^{e}}\,dz_{\mid P}^{\otimes e}.

Consequently, as a special case of Proposition
5.4.2
, we obtain:

Proposition 5.4.3
.

For any nonconstant holomorphic function

\alpha:\overline{D}(0,1)^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
of ramification index
e
at
0
,
we have:

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(\overline{D}(0,1),0)\rightarrow\mathbb{C})=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\log|\alpha(e^{2i\pi t_{1}})-\alpha(e^{2i\pi t_{2}})|\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}-\log|\alpha^{(e)}(0)/e!|.

Applied to
N=\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
and to the non-negative Green function
g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
defined by (
5.3.2
), Proposition
5.4.1
immediately implies:

Proposition 5.4.4
.

For every non-constant function
\alpha:V^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
of ramification index
e
at
P
, we have:

(5.4.9)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C}))\leq 2\int_{V}g_{V,P}\,\alpha^{\ast}\omega_{\rm{FS}}-\log\|\alpha^{[e]}(P)\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}.

When
\alpha
is a meromorphic function defined on some open disk
\mathring{D}(0,R)
, Proposition
5.4.4
applied with
V
a closed disc
\overline{D}(0,r)
contained in
\mathring{D}(0,R)
provides an upper-bound of the overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(\overline{D}(0,r),0)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C}))
in terms of the characteristic function
T_{\alpha}
of Nevanlinna-Ahlfors-Shimizu
4
4

4

See for instance
[
Ahl30
]
,
[
Ahl35
]
, and
[
Gri76
, Chapter 1]
.
attached to
\alpha
.

Proposition 5.4.5
.

Let
\alpha:\mathring{D}(0,R)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
be a non-constant meromorphic function for some
R
in
(0,+\infty]
. Assume that
\alpha(0)
is not
\infty
, and let
e
be the ramification index of
\alpha
at
0
. For every
r
in
(0,R),
the following estimate holds:

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(\overline{D}(0,r),0)\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C}))\leq 2\,T_{\alpha}(r)-e\log r-\log\frac{|\alpha^{(e)}(0)/e!|}{1+|\alpha(0)|^{2}}.

where:

(5.4.10)

T_{\alpha}(r):=\int_{\mathring{D}(0,r)}\log(r/|z|)\,\alpha^{\ast}\omega_{\rm{FS}}.

When
\alpha
is holomorphic on
\mathring{D}(0,R)
, a straightforward application of Green’s formula shows the the characteristic function
T_{\alpha}
admits the following expression:

T_{\alpha}(r)=(1/2)\int_{0}^{1}\log(1+|\alpha(re^{2\pi it})|^{2})\,dt-(1/2)\log(1+|\alpha(0)|^{2}).

Consequently, applied to a holomorphic function, Proposition
5.4.5
takes the following form:

Corollary 5.4.6
.

Let
\alpha:\mathring{D}(0,R)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be a non-constant holomorphic function for some
R
in
(0,+\infty]
, and let
e
be the ramification index of
\alpha
at
0
. For every
r
in
(0,R),
the following estimate holds:

(5.4.11)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(\overline{D}(0,r),0)\rightarrow\mathbb{C})\leq\int_{0}^{1}\log(1+|\alpha(re^{2\pi it})|^{2})\,dt-e\log r-\log|\alpha^{(e)}(0)/e!|.

The estimate (
5.4.11
) immediately implies the following one, which is also a simple consequence of Proposition
5.4.3
:

(5.4.12)

\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(\overline{D}(0,r),0)\rightarrow\mathbb{C})\leq 2\int_{0}^{1}\log^{+}|\alpha(re^{2\pi it})|\,dt+\log 2-e\log r-\log|\alpha^{(e)}(0)/e!|.

Example 5.4.7
.

Let
P\in\mathbb{C}[X]
be a polynomial of degree
d\geq 1
. We may write it:

P(X)=a_{d}X^{d}+\dots+a_{e}X^{e}+a_{0},

withe
1\leq e\leq d,
and
a_{d}
and
a_{e}
in
\mathbb{C}^{\times}.
From the definition (
5.2.2
) of the overflow or from its expression in Proposition
5.4.3
, one easily obtains:

\mathrm{Ex}(P:(\overline{D}(0,r)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}))=(d-e)\log r-\log|a_{e}/a_{d}|+o(1),\quad\mbox{when $r\longrightarrow+\infty$}.

This expression may be compared with the upper bound (
5.4.12
), which applied to
\alpha=P
takes the following form when
r
is large enough:

\mathrm{Ex}(P:(\overline{D}(0,r)\rightarrow\mathbb{C}))\leq(2d-e)\log r-\log|a_{e}/a_{d}^{2}|+\log 2.

Part III
Formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces, pseudoconcavity, and finiteness theorems

Chapter 6
Formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces and arithmetic intersection numbers

In this chapter, we introduce the arithmetic counterparts of the fibered (germs of) analytic surfaces studied in Chapter
2
in a geometric context. These are the
smooth formal-analytic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, with
K
some number field, already considered in
[
Bos20
, Chapter 10]
, and defined by gluing at the Archimedean places some “formal pointed curve over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
” — a typical instance of which is
{\rm Spf\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}[[T]]
— with connected compact Riemann surfaces with boundary.

Specifically
this chapter is devoted to diverse foundational results and to some basic examples concerning general smooth formal-analytic surfaces.

Notably we define the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
of regular and meromorphic functions on some smooth formal-analytic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. We introduce some rudiments of arithmetic intersection theory on these formal-analytic surfaces. The equilibrium potentials associated to the pointed compact Riemann surfaces with boundary defining
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
turn out to play a central role in this rudimentary intersection theory, which allows us to define the
pseudoconvexity
and the
pseudoconcavity
of a smooth formal-analytic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
.

We also discuss some simple examples of smooth formal-analytic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
, notably the smooth formal-analytic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
, which are defined by gluing
{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
to the closed disk
\overline{D}(0,1)
by means of some formal series
\psi
in the group:

G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R}):=\mathbb{R}^{\ast}X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]]

of “formal diffeomorphism of
\mathbb{R}
fixing
0
.”
These formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces turn out to play an important role in applications since these are precisely the smooth formal-analytic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
such that the underlying compact Riemann surface is simply connected.

In particular we prove that the algebra of regular functions on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
, namely:

{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)):=\left\{\widehat{\alpha}\in\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\mid\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}\mbox{ has a radius of convergence $>1$}\right\}

is “very large” when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
is pseudoconvex — namely when
|\psi^{\prime}(0)|>1
— and consists only of the constant functions with values in
\mathbb{Z}
when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
is pseudoconcave — namely when
|\psi^{\prime}(0)|<1
— and
\psi
is generic.
1
1

1

In Chapter
9
, we will prove that this algebra is always a
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra of finite type when
|\psi^{\prime}(0)|<1
.

Our proof of the “generic smallness” of
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi))
in the pseudoconcave case relies on some curious measure theoretic arguments involving the subgroup:

D(\mathbb{R}):=X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]],

of
G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R})
. Equipped with the topology induced by the natural topology of Fréchet space on
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
, defined by the simple convergence of coefficients,
D(\mathbb{R})
is a Polish group. Since it is not locally compact, it does not admit any non-trivial invariant Borel measure. However it contains:

D(\mathbb{Z}):=X+X^{2}\mathbb{Z}[[X]]

as a closed subgroup, and remarkably the quotient
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
is compact and admits a canonical probability measure
\bar{\mu}
that is invariant under the left action of
D(\mathbb{R})
.

The canonical measure
\bar{\mu}
on
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
turns out to be very useful when investigating the generic properties of the formal-analytic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
. Actually the isomorphism class of the formal-analytic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
depends only on the class of
\psi
in:

G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})\simeq(\mathbb{R}^{\ast}\ltimes D(\mathbb{R}))/D(\mathbb{Z}),

and, for a given value of
|\psi^{\prime}(0)|
, this isomorphism class is given by some element of the quotient
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
.

In this chapter, we denote by
K
a number field, and by
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
its ring of integers.

6.1.
Definitions

6.1.1.
Smooth formal-analytic surfaces

As in
[
Bos20
, 10.6.1]
, a
smooth formal-analytic arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, or shortly
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
,
is defined as a pair:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},O_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

where:

•

{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
is a Noetherian affine formal scheme over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\pi:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}=\mathrm{Spf}B\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

such that the restriction of
\pi
to the reduced scheme of definition
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
is an isomorphism:

(6.1.1)

\pi_{\mid|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|}:|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},

and such that the topological algebra
B=\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}})
is formally smooth over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and
\pi
has one-dimensional fibers;

•

for every complex embedding
\sigma
of
K
,
V_{\sigma}
is a connected compact Riemann surface with nonempty boundary,
2
2

2

in the sense of
0.6.2
; it would be more properly denoted by
(V_{\sigma},V_{\sigma}^{+})
.

O_{\sigma}
is a point in the interior
\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
of
V_{\sigma}
, and

\iota_{\sigma}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{V}_{\sigma,O_{\sigma}}

is an isomorphism between the smooth formal complex curve
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}:={\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\otimes_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}
, defined by the topological
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
B\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}
, and the formal completion
\widehat{V}_{\sigma,O_{\sigma}}
of
V_{\sigma}
at the point
O_{\sigma}
.

These data are moreover assumed to be compatible with complex conjugation; see
loc. cit.
. In particular, the compact Riemann surface with boundary:

V_{\mathbb{C}}:=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}V_{\sigma}

is endowed with a real structure, that is with an antiholomorphic involution — which we shall refer to as complex conjugation – which exchanges
(V_{\sigma},O_{\sigma})
and
(V_{\overline{\sigma}},O_{\overline{\sigma}})
. The isomorphism:

(\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}:\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\widehat{V}_{\sigma,O_{\sigma}}

is compatible with the canonical real structure on:

{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}:={\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}\simeq\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}

and the real structure on:

\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\widehat{V}_{\sigma,O_{\sigma}}

deduced from the real structure on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
.

The isomorphism inverse of (
6.1.1
) defines a section of
\pi,
that will be denoted:

P:{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}.

Endowed with
P,

{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
is a pointed smooth formal curve over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
in the sense of
[
Bos20
, 10.4.1]
.

For every field embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
the support
3
3

3

namely, the underlying topological space.
of the complex formal curve
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}
(resp.
\widehat{V}_{\sigma,O_{\sigma}}
) is the set
\{P_{\sigma}\}
, (resp. the set
\{O_{\sigma}\}
), and the isomorphism
\iota_{\sigma}
sends
P_{\sigma}
to
O_{\sigma}
. It will be notationally convenient to identify these two complex points, and from now on, we will write
P_{\sigma}
instead of
O_{\sigma}
.

If
\mathcal{I}
denotes the largest ideal of definition of
B
, we define the
normal line bundle
of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as:

N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}:=P^{*}(\mathcal{I}/\mathcal{I}^{2}).

It is an invertible sheaf over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and there exists a (non-canonical) isomorphism of topological
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebras:

B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathrm{Sym}}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}(N_{{\mathcal{P}}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})^{\vee};

see
[
Bos20
, Section 10.4]
for more details.

The smooth formal-analytic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
will often be refered to as
smooth formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces
when the base scheme
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
is clear from the context, or, for short, as
smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
.

6.1.2.
Vector bundles and spaces of sections

As discussed in
[
Bos20
, 10.6.1]
, there is a natural notion of a

vector bundle
:

\widetilde{E}:=(\widehat{E},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}),

and of a
Hermitian vector bundle
:

(6.1.2)

\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=(\widehat{E},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

over a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as above.

These are defined by a vector bundle
\widehat{E}
over the formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, a complex analytic vector bundle
E_{\sigma}
(resp. a Hermitian vector bundle
(E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})
) over the Riemann surface with boundary
4
4

4

See
0.6.2
for the definitions of complex analytic and Hermitian vector bundles over Riemann surfaces with boundary.

V_{\sigma}
, and by some gluing data
(\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
that consists of isomorphisms:

\varphi_{\sigma}:\widehat{E}_{\sigma}:=\widehat{E}\otimes_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\iota_{\sigma}^{*}\big(E_{\sigma\mid\widehat{V}_{\sigma,P_{\sigma}}}\big)

of vector bundles over the complex formal curves
{\mathcal{V}}_{\sigma}.

These data are assumed to be compatible with complex conjugation.

The usual tensor operations — such as the direct sum, the tensor product, or the exterior powers — make sense for Hermitian vector bundles over smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces, and satisfy formal properties similar to the properties of Hermitian vector bundles over reduced analytic spaces or over reduced schemes of finite type over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
.

In particular, to any smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as above, we may attach the Picard group
\overline{{\rm Pic}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, defined as the set of isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, endowed with the commutative group law defined by the tensor product. We may also introduce its subgroup
\overline{{\rm Pic}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
defined by those Hermitian line bundles whose metrics are restriction to
V_{\mathbb{C}}
of metric with
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
regularity on:

V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}:=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}V_{\sigma}^{+}.

Consider a formal-analytic arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
and a Hermitian vector bundle
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as above. We assume that the Riemann surface
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, is endowed with
a positive smooth volume forme
\mu

invariant under complex conjugation, and, for every field embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we let:

\mu_{\sigma}:=\mu_{|V_{\sigma}}.

To
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
,
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
, and
\mu
is associated a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, in the sense of
[
Bos20
, Chapter 5]
:

(6.1.3)

\Gamma_{L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}}):=\Big(\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E}),(\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma}),\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Big),

see
[
Bos20
, 10.6.2]
. Here
\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})
denotes the Hilbert space of holomorphic sections
s
of
E_{\sigma}
over
\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
such that

(6.1.4)

\|s\|_{\sigma,L^{2}}^{2}:=\int_{V_{\sigma}}\|s(x)\|^{2}_{\sigma}d\mu_{\sigma}(x)

is finite, and
\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}
is the “Taylor series expansion”:

\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}:\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\sigma,P_{\sigma}},E_{\sigma})\simeq\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma,P_{\sigma}},\widehat{E}_{\sigma})\simeq\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E})\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}

that sends a
L^{2}
holomorphic section
s
of
E_{\sigma}
over
\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
to its jet of infinite order at
P_{\sigma}
.

In this monograph, we shall use the notation:

(6.1.5)

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=\Gamma_{L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}})

for the pro-Hermitian vector bundle defined by (
6.1.3
). When it is
\theta
-finite, as defined in
[
Bos20
, 7.7.2]
, we shall denote its
{h^{0}_{\theta}}
-invariant by:

(6.1.6)

{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}}):={h^{0}_{\theta}}\Big(\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}\Big)\quad(\in\mathbb{R}_{+}).

This non-negative real number may be seen as an arithmetic analogue of the dimension of the space of analytic (resp. regular) sections of an analytic vector bundle (resp. of a vector bundle) over a germ of analytic surface (resp. over a formal scheme) fibered over a smooth projective curve, as considered in Chapter
2
.

The new notation (
6.1.5
) is intended to avoid any confusion with the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-module:

\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\widetilde{E}):=\Big\{(\widehat{s},(s_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})\in\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E})\times\prod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{+},E_{\sigma})\,\big|\,\forall\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},\,\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}(s_{\sigma})=\widehat{s}{\otimes}_{\sigma}1\Big\}

attached to a vector bundle
\widetilde{E}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
; here
\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}
denotes again the Taylor series expansion at
P_{\sigma}
:

\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}:\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{+},E_{\sigma})\longrightarrow\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E})\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C},

on the space of sections of
E_{\sigma}
that are analytic up to the boundary of
V_{\sigma}
.

The projection maps:

\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\widetilde{E})\longrightarrow\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E})

and:

\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\widetilde{E})\longrightarrow\Gamma(V_{\sigma}^{+},E_{\sigma})

are injective, and the elements of
\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\widetilde{E})
may be described as those sections of the formal vector bundle
\widehat{E}
on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
that extend to sections of the vector bundles
E_{\sigma}
, analytic up to the boundary of the Riemann surfaces
V_{\sigma}
. Observe that this condition of analyticity on
V^{+}_{\sigma}
is stronger than the analyticity over
\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
together with the finiteness of (
6.1.4
) which enters in the definition of
\Gamma_{L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}}).

The construction of the direct image pro-Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}
associated to a Hermitian vector bundle
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
admits the following minor generalization which will be useful in the next chapters.

We may define a
Hermitian torsion free coherent sheaf

\widetilde{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as a pair (
6.1.2
), where the

(E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})
) are still Hermitian vector bundles over the Riemann surfaces with boundary
V_{\sigma}
, but where now
{\widehat{E}}
is a torsion free coherent sheaf on the formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
.

Recall that, for every torsion free coherent sheaf
{\widehat{E}}
on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, the bidual coherent sheaf
{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee}
is a vector bundle over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, the biduality morphism:

\iota:{\widehat{E}}\longrightarrow{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee}

is an injective morphism of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}
-modules, and its cokernel
{\rm coker\,}\iota
is a coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
-module supported by a finite set of closed points in
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|\simeq{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

In other words,
{\rm coker\,}\iota
is the
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}
-module associated to a
finite

{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
-module
C
. In particular, for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, the vectors bundles
{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee}_{\sigma}
and
{\widehat{E}}_{\sigma}
on the formal curve
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}
may be identified.

Associated to the vector bundle
{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee}
over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, we may define the Hermitian vector bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
:

(6.1.7)

{\widetilde{\overline{E}}}^{\vee\vee}:=({\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

The short exact sequence of topological
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-modules:

(6.1.8)

0\longrightarrow\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},{\widehat{E}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee})\longrightarrow C\longrightarrow 0

shows that
\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},{\widehat{E}})
, like
\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},{\widehat{E}}^{\vee\vee})
, is an object of the category
\mathbf{CTC}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}
, with the notation of
[
Bos20
, Chapter 4]
. Since the direct image of
\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee}
, namely:

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee}:=\Big(\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E}),(\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma}),\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Big),

is a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
this shows that the direct image of
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
, defined as:

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=\Big(\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\widehat{E}^{\vee\vee}),(\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma}),\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Big),

is also a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

Actually the short exact sequence (
6.1.8
) show that the properties of the pro-Hermitian vector bundles
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee}
and
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}
are closely related. For instance, we have:

Proposition 6.1.1
.

For every
Hermitian torsion free coherent sheaf
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, the pro-Hermitian vector bundles
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee}
is
\theta
-finite if and only if
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}
is
\theta
-finite. When this holds, using the notation (
6.1.6
), their
\theta
-invariants satisfy the following estimates:

(6.1.9)

{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee})-\log|C|\leq{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}})\leq{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\widetilde{\overline{E}}^{\vee\vee}).

Proposition
6.1.1
is a direct consequence of the following result of independent interest concerning pro-Hermitian vector bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

Proposition 6.1.2
.

Let
{\widehat{\overline{F}}}:=({\widehat{F}},(F^{\mathrm{Hilb}}_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma},i_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
be a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. If
{\widehat{F}}^{\prime}
is an open
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-submodule of finite index in
{\widehat{F}},
then:

(6.1.10)

{\widehat{\overline{F}}}^{\prime}:=({\widehat{F}}^{\prime},(F^{\mathrm{Hilb}}_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma},i_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

is a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. Moreover
{\widehat{\overline{F}}}^{\prime}
is
\theta
-finite if and only if
{\widehat{\overline{F}}}
is, and when this holds, the following estimates are satisfied:

{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widehat{\overline{F}}}^{\prime})\leq{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widehat{\overline{F}}})\leq{h^{0}_{\theta}}({\widehat{\overline{F}}}^{\prime})+\log|{\widehat{F}}/{\widehat{F}}^{\prime}|.

Observe that the right-hand side of (
6.1.10
) indeed defines a pro-Hermitian vector bundle since, for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
{\widehat{F}}^{\prime}_{\sigma}:={\widehat{F}}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}
may be identified to
{\widehat{F}}_{\sigma}:={\widehat{F}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}
by the completed base change of the inclusion map
{\widehat{F}}^{\prime}\hookrightarrow{\widehat{F}}.

Proposition
6.1.2
is a simple consequence of its special case when
{\widehat{\overline{F}}}
is a Hermitian vector bundle, established in
[
Bos20
, Corollary 2.3.4]
, and of the characterizations of strongly summable and
\theta
-finite pro-Hermitian vector bundles in
[
Bos20
, Section 7.7]
.

6.1.3.
The algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})

We may apply the construction of the space of sections
\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\widetilde{E})
to the vector bundle
\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
defined by:

\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}:=(\mathcal{O}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}},(\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{an}}_{V_{\sigma}},\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}),

where
\varphi_{\sigma}
is the tautological isomorphism:

\mathcal{O}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},\sigma}\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\iota_{\sigma}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widehat{V}_{\sigma,P_{\sigma}}}.

This defines an
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra:

\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):=\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mathcal{O}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})\subseteq{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})\times\prod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{O}}^{{\rm an}}(V_{\sigma}^{+}),

the algebra of
regular functions on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
,

whose elements may be described as those formal functions on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
that extend to functions analytic up to the boundary on the Riemann surfaces
V_{\sigma}
.
It is readily seen that the canonical morphism:

{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),\quad a\longmapsto\left(\pi^{\ast}a,(\sigma(a))_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\right)

is injective, and that
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is an integrally closed domain.

In the same spirit, we may consider the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
of formal meromorphic functions on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, as defined in
[
HM68
]
,
5
5

5

The field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
coincides with the fraction field of the domain
{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}):=\Gamma(|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}})
. For instance, when
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}=\mathbb{Z}
, the formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
is isomorphic to
{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
and
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
to
\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
.
and the fields
{\mathcal{M}}(V_{\sigma}^{+})
of meromorphic functions up to the boundary on the Riemann surfaces
V_{\sigma}
, and define the field of
meromorphic functions on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as follows:

{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):=\Big\{(\hat{f},(f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})\in{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})\times\prod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{M}}(V_{\sigma}^{+})\,\big|\,\forall\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},\,\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}(f_{\sigma})=\hat{f}{\otimes}_{\sigma}1\Big\}.

Here
\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}
denotes the Laurent expansion at
P_{\sigma}
:

\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}:{\mathcal{M}}(V_{\sigma}^{+})\longrightarrow\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}^{\rm an}_{V_{\sigma}\!,P_{\sigma}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{V}_{\sigma}\!,P_{\sigma}},

and
\hat{f}\otimes_{\sigma}\!1
is an element of the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})\otimes_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\!,\sigma}\mathbb{C}
, which is canonically embedded in
\mathrm{Frac}\,(\mathcal{O}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\!,\sigma}\mathbb{C})
, which in turn is isomorphic to

\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{V}_{\sigma}\!,P_{\sigma}}

by
\iota_{\sigma}^{\ast}
. If
z
denotes a local analytic coordinate on some neighborhood of
P_{\sigma}
in
\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
that vanishes at
P_{\sigma},
the field
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}^{\mathrm{an}}_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{V}}_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}
) may be identified with the field of Laurent series
\mathbb{C}\{z\}[z^{-1}]
(resp.
\mathbb{C}[[z]][z^{-1}]
).

In brief, an element of the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is a formal meromorphic function on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
that extends to meromorphic functions up to the boundary on the Riemann surfaces
V_{\sigma}^{+}
.

The
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
naturally embeds into
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, which is therefore an extension of the fraction field
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, hence of the number field
K
.

6.2.
Arakelov divisors and intersection numbers on f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces

In this section, we still denote by
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},O_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface as defined in
6.1.1
.

6.2.1.
The group
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})

We define an
Arakelov divisor with compact support
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
as a pair
(D,g)
where:

•

D
is a
1
-dimensional algebraic cycle on
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
;

•

g
is
a Green function (with
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
regularity) for the divisor
D_{\mathbb{C}}
on the Riemann surface
V^{+}

that is invariant under complex conjugation, and whose support satisfies:

(6.2.1)

{\mathrm{supp}\,}g\subseteq V_{\mathbb{C}}:=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}V_{\sigma}.

The first condition on
D
simply means that
D
is of the form:

D=nP

for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}.

For every field embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, we shall use the notation:

g_{\sigma}:=g_{\mid V_{\sigma}^{+}}.

The support condition (
6.2.1
) on
g
is equivalent to the vanishing of the each of these functions
g_{\sigma}
on
V^{+}_{\sigma}\setminus\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
. In particular the restriction
g_{\sigma\mid V_{\mathbb{C}}}
of
g_{\sigma}
to the the compact submanifold
V_{\sigma}
satisfies the “Dirichlet boundary condition”:

g_{\mid\partial V_{\sigma}}=0.

The Arakelov divisors with compact supports on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
define an additive group which will be denoted by
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}).
There is a canonical morphism of commutative groups:

(6.2.2)

{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\longrightarrow\overline{{\rm Pic}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),

that maps the Arakelov divisor
(D,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
to the isomorphism class of the Hermitian line bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
:

\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(D,g):=({\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(D),({\mathcal{O}}^{{\rm an}}_{V^{+}_{\sigma}}(D_{\sigma}),\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}},\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}),

where the isomorphism
\varphi_{\sigma}
is the tautological isomorphism induced by
\iota_{\sigma}
.

Let us emphasize that the properties of the morphism (
6.2.2
) differ strikingly from those of the analogue morphism:

{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}(X)\longrightarrow\overline{{\rm Pic}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}}(X),

relating Arakelov divisors and Hermitian line bundles on a regular projective arithmetic surface
X
.
For instance, when
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}=\mathbb{Z}
, the morphism (
6.2.2
) is easily seen to be injective. Moreover its image is expected to be a “very small” subgroup of
\overline{{\rm Pic}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
belongs to the class of pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces, which are investigated in the next chapters.

6.2.2.
The Arakelov divisor
(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
and the line bundle
{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}

The divisor

P_{\mathbb{C}}:=\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}P_{\sigma}

in
V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
admits a distinguished Green function
{g}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}
that satisfies the support condition (
6.2.1
), namely the Green function defined by the equilibrium potentials for the points
P_{\sigma}
in the connected compact Riemann surfaces with boundary
V_{\sigma}
:

g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\mid V_{\sigma}^{+}}:=g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}},

introduced in
4.3.1
above.

The Green function
g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}
is invariant under complex conjugation, and the pair
(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
defines an element of
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, canonically attached to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
.

To this Arakelov divisor is attached the Hermitian line bundle
\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
. Its restriction to
P=|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
may be identified with the Hermitian line bundle:

(6.2.3)

\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=\left(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\right)

defined by the normal bundle
N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
of
P
in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
equipped with the capacitary metrics
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}
on the complex lines:

(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle T_{\iota_{\sigma}}}}{{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}}}N_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma}=T_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma},

introduced in
3.2.1
and
4.2.1
above.

The group
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
admits a simple description in terms of the Arakelov divisor
(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
.

Let us define
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
as the subspace of
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}})
consisting in the
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
-functions
\varphi:V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
that are invariant under complex conjugation acting on
V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
and satisfy the following “Dirichlet boundary condition:”

{\mathrm{supp}\,}\varphi\subseteq V_{\mathbb{C}}.

The following proposition is then a straightforward consequence of the definitions:

Proposition 6.2.1
.

The morphism of commutative groups:

\mathbb{Z}\oplus{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}\longrightarrow{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),\quad(n,\varphi)\longmapsto n(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})+(0,\varphi)

is an isomorphism.

6.2.3.
Arithmetic intersection theory on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}

Let
{\overline{L}}
be a Hermitian line bundle on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
. Its restriction to
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|=P
is a Hermitian line bundle on a one-dimensional scheme proper over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
, and admits a well defined arithmetic degree:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,({\overline{L}}|P)=0pt_{{\overline{L}}}(|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|):=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{L}}.

More generally, a divisor
D
in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
supported by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
may be written
D=nP,
with
P\in\mathbb{Z},
and we have:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,({\overline{L}}|D):=n\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{L}}.

For every Hermitian line bundle
\overline{L}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, defined by a metric of regularity
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
, and any Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, we may define their arithmetic intersection number by the same formula as in Subsections
3.3.2
and
4.5.2
:

(6.2.4)

\overline{L}\cdot(D,g):=\widehat{\rm deg}\,({\overline{L}}|D)+\int_{V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,c_{1}(\overline{L}_{\mathbb{C}})\quad\in\mathbb{R}.

The product
g\,c_{1}(\overline{L}_{\mathbb{C}})
is a product of a function in
C^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}})
and of a measure in
{\mathbf{M}}^{{\mathrm{cp}}}(V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}})
, and is supported by the compact submanifold
V_{\mathbb{C}}
. The integral in the right-hand side of (
6.2.4
) is therefore well-defined, as discussed in
4.2.2
, and could also be written:

\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,c_{1}(\overline{L}_{\mathbb{C}}).

We may specialize the definition (
6.2.4
) of arithmetic intersection numbers to Hermitian line bundles whose isomorphism class belong to the image of the morphism (
6.2.2
). Thus we define, for any two Arakelov divisors
(D,g)
and
(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
:

(6.2.5)

(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})\cdot(D,g):=\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})\cdot(D,g)=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(D^{\prime},g^{\prime})|D)+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,\omega(g^{\prime}).

Observe that, for every
(D,g)\in{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and any
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}},
we have:

\displaystyle(0,\varphi)\cdot(D,g)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(0,\varphi)\mid D)+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,\frac{i}{\pi}\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi

(6.2.6)

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}\varphi\,\delta_{D_{\mathbb{C}}}+\pi^{-1}\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi.

In particular, for every
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
, we have:

(6.2.7)

(0,\varphi_{1})\cdot(0,\varphi_{2})=-\pi^{-1}\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle_{\rm Dir}.

The fact that the measure
\omega(g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is supported by
\partial V_{\mathbb{C}}
implies that arithmetic intersection numbers involving the Arakelov divisor
(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
admit simple expressions:

Proposition 6.2.2
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and every Arakelov divisor of the form
(nP,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, we have:

(6.2.8)

(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot(nP,g)=n\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}.

In particular:

(6.2.9)

(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},

and, for every
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}},
we have:

(6.2.10)

(0,\varphi)\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=0.

Proof.

According to the definition (
6.2.5
) of the arithmetic intersection number, we have:

(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot(nP,g)=n\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,\omega(g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}).

As observed in
6.2.2
, the restriction of
\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is canonically isomorphic to
\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, and therefore:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}.

Moreover the product
g\,\omega(g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is zero. This establishes (
6.2.8
).
The relations (
6.2.9
) and (
6.2.10
) are special cases of (
6.2.8
).
∎

Corollary 6.2.3
.

For every
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
in
\mathbb{Z}
and
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
, we have:

(6.2.11)

(n_{1}P,n_{1}\,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\varphi_{1})\cdot(n_{2}P,n_{2}\,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\varphi_{2})=n_{1}n_{2}\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}-\pi^{-1}\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle_{\rm Dir}.

In particular, the arithmetic intersection pairing on
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
defined by (
6.2.5
) is symmetric. Moreover, for every
\varphi
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
, we have:

(6.2.12)

(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\varphi)\cdot(P,\,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}+\varphi)=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}-\pi^{-1}\langle\varphi,\varphi\rangle_{\rm Dir}.

This shows that the self-intersection of an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
of the form
(P,h)
satisfies:

(6.2.13)

(P,h)\cdot(P,h)\geq(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}),

and that equality holds in (
6.2.13
) if and only if
h=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}
.

6.3.
Pseudoconcavity and pseudoconvexity

The next two propositions establish an analogue, concerning f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces, of the results about
\mathbf{CNB}
divisors on analytic surfaces fibered over a projective curve in Proposition
2.2.1
and Corollary
2.2.2
.

Proposition 6.3.1
.

Let
h
be a Green function
h
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
in
V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that
(P,h)
belongs to
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
.

The following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

for every
(D,g)\in{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
:

D\geq 0\quad\mbox{and}\quad g\geq 0\,\,\Longrightarrow(P,h)\cdot(D,g)\geq 0;

(2)

\omega(h)_{\mid\mathring{V}_{\mathbb{C}}}\geq 0\mbox{ and }\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)\geq 0.

Proof.

Let us assume that
(i)
holds. Then, for every non-negative function
\psi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}},
we have:

0\leq(P,h)\cdot(0,\psi)=\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}\psi\,\omega(h).

This is readily seen to imply that, over
\mathring{V}_{\mathbb{C}}
, the measure
\omega(h)
is non-negative.

Moreover, for any non-negative Green function
g
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
in
V_{\mathbb{C}}^{+}
such that
(P,g)
belongs to
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, we have:

(6.3.1)

0\leq(P,h)\cdot(P,g)=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,\omega(h).

Clearly there exists such a non-negative Green function
g,
and the construction in
4.2.2
of
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
functions approximating a Green function with
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
regularity establishes the existence of a sequence
(g_{n})
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
such that:

0\leq g_{n}\leq g,

and:

\lim_{n\rightarrow+\infty}\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}(g-g_{n})\,\omega(h)=0.

The inequality (
6.3.1
), applied to
g-g_{n}
instead of
g
, implies:

0\leq\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}(g-g_{n})\,\omega(h),

and by letting
n
go to infinity, we obtain:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)\geq 0.

Conversely, assume that (ii) holds, and let
(D,g)
be an element of
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
such that
D\geq 0
and
g\geq 0.
Then
D=nP
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and:

(P,h)\cdot(D,g)=(P,h)\cdot(nP,g)=n\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g\,\omega(h)

is clearly non-negative.
∎

Let us denote :

\mathcal{SH}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}:=\left\{\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}\mid i\partial\overline{\partial}\varphi_{\mid\mathring{V}_{\mathbb{C}}}\geq 0\right\}.

The functions in
\mathcal{SH}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
are continuous on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, subharmonic on
\mathring{V}_{\mathbb{C}}
, and vanish on
\partial V_{\mathbb{C}}.
Consequently, they are non-positive on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proposition 6.3.2
.

The following two conditions are equivalent:

(1)

There exists a Green function
h
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
in
V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that
(P,h)
belongs to
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and such that, for every
(D,g)\in{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
:

(6.3.2)

D\geq 0\quad\mbox{and}\quad g\geq 0\,\,\Longrightarrow(P,h)\cdot(D,g)\geq 0.

(2)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\geq 0.

When these conditions are satisfied, the set of Green functions
h
as in (i) is
g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}+{\mathcal{C}}
, where:

{\mathcal{C}}:=\Big\{\varphi\in\mathcal{SH}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}\mid\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\varphi(P_{\sigma})+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\geq 0\Big\}.

In particular, (i) holds with
h=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{C}}
, and every Green function
h
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
as in (i) satisfies:
h\leq g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}.

Since
g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{C}}
is non-negative, the conditions (i) and (ii) are also equivalent to the strengthened variant of (i) where
h
is required to be non-negative.

Proof.

The Green functions
h
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
such that
(P,h)
belongs to
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
are precisely of the form:

h=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{C}}+\varphi

with
\varphi
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}
. When this holds, we have:

\omega(h)_{\mid\mathring{V}_{\mathbb{C}}}\geq 0\Longleftrightarrow\varphi\in\mathcal{SH}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}},

and:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widetilde{\overline{\mathcal{O}}}(P,h)|P)=\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}\varphi\,\delta_{P_{\mathbb{C}}}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}=\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\varphi(P_{\sigma})+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}.

The proposition therefore follows from Proposition
6.3.1
and from the non-positivity of the functions in
\mathcal{SH}(V_{\mathbb{C}})_{\mathbb{R},{\rm Dir}}.

∎

Definition 6.3.3
.

We shall say that the smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is
pseudoconcave
(resp.
pseudoconvex
) when:

(6.3.3)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}>0\quad\mbox{( resp. $\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}<0$)}.

Proposition
6.3.2
and the inequality (
6.2.13
) show that
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is pseudoconcave if and only if there exists a non-negative Green function
h
for
P_{\mathbb{C}}
in
V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}
such that
(P,h)
belongs to
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and satisfies the condition of “numerical effectivity” (
6.3.2
) and the “bigness” condition:

(P,h)\cdot(P,h)>0.

Moreover, when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is pseudoconcave, these conditions are satisfied by
h=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}.

6.4.
The arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)

In this section, we discuss some simple examples of smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
and of the associated algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
. Our aim is to demonstrate, on some simple but significant examples, the crucial role played the numerical conditions (
6.3.3
) on the “size” of the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
.

For simplicity, we restrict ourselves to f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces over
\mathbb{Z}
, and leave it to the interested reader to extend the results of this sections when
\mathbb{Z}
is replaced by the ring
{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
of integers of an arbitrary number field
K
.

Indeed, when
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}=\mathbb{Z},
the definitions in
6.1.1
and
6.1.3
above become especially simple. Namely, a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}=(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}},(V,O,i))
over
\mathbb{Z}
is the data of a formal scheme
\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
, isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
, of a connected compact Riemann surface with non-empty boundary
V
equipped with a real structure, of a real point
O
in
{\mathring{V}}
, and of an isomorphism of complex formal curves, compatible with the real structures:

i:\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{V}_{O}.

The section
P:{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
is the inverse of the tautological isomorphism
\big|\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[X]]\big|\simeq{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
.

Moreover the elements of the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
(resp. of the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
) may be described as pairs
\alpha:=(\hat{\alpha},\alpha^{{\rm an}})
where
\hat{\alpha}
and
\alpha^{{\rm an}}
belong respectively to
{\mathcal{O}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}})
and
{\mathcal{O}}^{{\rm an}}(V^{+})
(resp. to
{\mathcal{M}}(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}})\simeq\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
and
{\mathcal{M}}(V^{+})
) and satisfy the following gluing condition:

i^{\ast}\alpha^{{\rm an}}_{\mid\widehat{V}_{O}}=\hat{\alpha}_{C}.

6.4.1.
The f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(D,\psi)

Let
D
be a compact domain with
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
boundary
6
6

6

that is, a compact
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
submanifold with boundary, of dimension 2.
in
\mathbb{C}
, that is invariant under complex conjugation and contains
0
in its interior, and let
\psi
be a formal series in
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
such that:

(6.4.1)

\psi(0)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\psi^{\prime}(0)\neq 0.

To
(D,\psi)
, we may attach the smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}=(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}},(V,P,i))
over
\mathbb{Z}
defined as follows:

\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}}:=\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[X]],\quad V:=D,\quad O=0,

and:
7
7

7

In other words, for any
f\in\mathbb{C}[[z]]\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{\hat{D}_{0}},
i^{\ast}f:=f\circ\psi.

i:=\psi:\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{C}[[X]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{Spf\,}\mathbb{C}[[z]]\simeq\widehat{D}_{0}.

We shall denote it by
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(D,\psi)
.

By definition, the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(D,\psi))
may be identified with the subring of formal series
\hat{\alpha}
in
\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
such that
\hat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}
is the Taylor expansion in
0
of an analytic function on some open neighborhood of
D
.

Similarly, the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(D,\psi))
may be identified with the field of Laurent series
\widehat{\alpha}
in
\mathbb{Q}((X))
that belong to its subfield
\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
, such that
\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}
is the Taylor expansion in
0
of an meromorphic function on some open neighborhood of
D
.

For instance, for any
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\ast}
, we may define the
Borel f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface of radius
r
, denoted by
\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r),
by the above construction applied to
D:=\overline{D}(0;r)
, the closed disk of radius
r
, and to the “identity” series
\psi=X
:

\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r):={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;r),X).

The equilibrium potential for
P_{\mathbb{C}}=0
in
\overline{D}(0;r)
is the function:

g_{\overline{D}(0;r),0}=(z\mapsto\log^{+}|z/r|),

and therefore the associated capacitary metric
\|.\|_{\overline{D}(0;r),0}^{\mathrm{cap}}
satisfies:

\|\partial/\partial z\|_{\overline{D}(0;r),0}^{\mathrm{cap}}=r^{-1}.

Consequently the Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{N}}_{P}\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)
over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
may be identified with:

(\mathbb{Z}\;\partial/\partial X,\|\partial/\partial X\|=r^{-1}),

and therefore satisfies:

(6.4.2)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,N_{P}\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)=\log r.

The “size” of the ring
{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r))
clearly depends on the sign of (
6.4.2
). Indeed when

\widehat{\rm deg}\,N_{P}\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)\geq 0,

or equivalently when
r\geq 1
, it is easily seen to be reduced to the ring of polynomials:

{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r))=\mathbb{Z}[X].

In contrast, when
\widehat{\rm deg}\,N_{P}\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)<0
, that is when
r<1
, the ring
{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r))
is “very large”: it contains all formal series in
\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
with bounded coefficients and therefore has the cardinality of the continuum.

More generally, to
any formal series
\psi
in
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
satisfying the conditions (
6.4.1
), we may attach the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)
.
When
\psi=X/r
, it is isomorphic to the Borel f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)
.

The Hermitian line bundle
N_{{\mathcal{P}}}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)
may be identified with
(\mathbb{Z}\,\partial/\partial X,\|.\|_{\psi})
where the metric
\|.\|_{\psi}
satisfies:

\|\psi^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\partial/\partial X\|_{\psi}=1.

Consequently:

(6.4.3)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,N_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)=\log|\psi^{\prime}(0)|^{-1}.

According to the uniformization theorem, every simply connected pointed compact Riemann surface with boundary
(V^{+},P)
is isomorphic to
(\overline{D}(0,1)^{+},0)
. This implies that a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
is isomorphic to a f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)
for some formal series
\psi
in the group:

G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R}):=\mathbb{R}^{\ast}X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]]

if and only if the underlying compact Riemann with boundary is simply connected.

6.4.2.
The arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi)
: the pseudoconvex case

In this subsection, we want to prove that, when the arithmetic degree (
6.4.3
) is negative, the ring of regular functions
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi))
is large. This will extend the discussion above concerning the ring
{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r))
associated to the Borel f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
