\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r)
when
r<1
.

To achieve this, we will rely on the following
elementary proposition:

Proposition 6.4.1
.

Let
\psi
be a formal series in
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
such that:

\psi(0)=0\quad\mbox{and}\quad\lambda:=\psi^{\prime}(0)\neq 0.

For every integer
e\geq 1,
there exists a formal series in
\mathbb{Z}[[X]]
:

\widehat{\alpha}=X^{e}+\sum_{n\geq e+1}\alpha_{n}X^{n}

such that the coefficients of the formal series in
\mathbb{R}[[T]]
:

\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}(T)=:\lambda^{-e}\;T^{e}+\sum_{n\geq e+1}a_{n}T^{n}

satisfy the estimates:

(6.4.4)

|a_{n}|\leq|\lambda|^{-n}/2\quad\mbox{for every $n\geq e+1$.}

Proof.

For every
n\geq e+1
, the
n
-th coefficients
a_{n}
of the formal series
\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}
may be written:

a_{n}=\lambda^{-n}\alpha_{n}+P_{n}(\alpha_{e+1},\dots,\alpha_{n-1}),

where
P_{n}
denotes some polynomial with real coefficients in
n-e-1
variables, depending of
e
and
\psi
; in particular
P_{e+1}
is a constant depending only on
e
and
\psi
. We may construct the coefficients
(\alpha_{n})_{n\geq e+1}
inductively by choosing
\alpha_{n}
as an integer such that:

|\alpha_{n}+\lambda^{n}\,P_{n}(a_{e+1},\dots,a_{n-1},\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{n})|\leq 1/2.

This immediately implies the estimates (
6.4.4
)
∎

We will use the following straightforward consequence of the estimates (
6.4.4
):

Corollary 6.4.2
.

The radius of convergence of the series
\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}(T)
is at least
|\lambda|
. When
|\lambda|>1,
the series
\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}
defines a complex analytic function on some open neighborhood of
\overline{D}(0;1)
; moreover:

(6.4.5)

\max_{z\in\overline{D}(0;1)}|\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}(z)|\leq|\lambda|^{-e}+\sum_{n\geq e+1}|\lambda|^{-n}/2=\frac{1-|\lambda|^{-1}/2}{1-|\lambda|^{-1}}\,|\lambda|^{-e}.

Corollary
6.4.2
shows that, when
|\lambda|>1
, the series
\hat{\alpha}
defines a non-constant element
\alpha:=(\widehat{\alpha},\alpha^{{\rm an}})
in the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0;1),\psi))
. If
e
is chosen large enough, the image

\alpha^{{\rm an}}(\overline{D}(0;1)):=\widehat{\alpha}\circ\psi^{-1}(\overline{D}(0;1))

is a compact subset of the open disc
\mathring{D}(0;1)
.

The previous construction therefore establishes the following proposition:

Proposition 6.4.3
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=(\widehat{{\mathcal{V}}},(V,P,i))
be a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
\mathbb{Z}
such that
V
is simply connected. If it satisfies the condition:

(6.4.6)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}<0,

then the algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
contains a non-constant element
\alpha:=(\widehat{\alpha},\alpha^{{\rm an}})
such that
\widehat{\alpha}(P)=0
and
\alpha^{{\rm an}}(V)
is contained in
\mathring{D}(0;1)
.

Observe that the conclusion of Proposition
6.4.3
implies that the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is large. Indeed, for
\alpha
as in the conclusion of Proposition
6.4.3
, for every
r\in(0,1)
such that:

\alpha^{{\rm an}}(V)\subseteq\overline{D}(0;r),

we may define an injective morphism of rings:

{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}(r))\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),\quad\beta:=(\widehat{\beta},\beta^{\rm an})\longmapsto\beta\circ\alpha:=(\widehat{\beta}\circ\widehat{\alpha},\beta^{\rm an}\circ\alpha^{\rm an}).

It is likely that Proposition
6.4.3
still holds for every smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
— where
K
denotes an arbitrary number field — that satisfies the pseudoconcavity condition (
6.4.6
). This would constitute an arithmetic analogue of the theorem of Grauert concerning pseudoconvex neighborhoods of projective curves discussed in
1.4.3
above.

6.4.3.
The arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
: the pseudoconcave case

In this subsection and in the next section, we are going to show that, if
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies:

0<|\lambda|<1,

then for most choices of the formal series
\psi
in
\lambda X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]],
the algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
is as small as possible, namely is reduced to
\mathbb{Z}
.

To formulate our results, we need to introduce some notation.

For any commutative ring
A,
we may consider the subset:

D(A):=X+X^{2}A[[X]]

of the algebra of formal series
A[[X]]
. Endowed with the composition
\circ
of formal series with vanishing constant terms, it defines a group
(D(A),\circ)
. For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we may consider its normal subgroup:

H_{n}(A):=X+X^{n+2}A[[X]].

The quotient group:

D_{n}(A):=D(A)/H_{n}(A)

may be identified with the group
X+X^{2}A[[X]]/(X^{n+2})
of formal series truncated at order
n+1,
endowed with the composition. The groups
D_{n}(A)
are nilpotent, and
D(A)
may be identified with the limit:

D(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\varprojlim_{n}D_{n}(A)

of the following diagram of surjective morphisms of groups:

D_{0}(A)=\{e\}\longleftarrow D_{1}(A)\longleftarrow\cdots\longleftarrow D_{n}(A)\longleftarrow D_{n+1}(A)\longleftarrow\cdots.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N},

D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
is a nilpotent Lie group of dimension
n
.
We shall denote by
\mu_{n}
the measure on
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
image of the Lebesgue measure by the diffeomorphism:

\iota_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D_{n}(\mathbb{R}),\quad(a_{2},\dots,a_{n+1})\longmapsto X+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}a_{i}X^{i}\mod X^{n+2}.

It is easily seen to be a left and right Haar measure on the Lie group
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N},
the discrete subgroup
D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})
of
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
is cocompact, and the map

[0,1)^{n}\longrightarrow D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z}),\quad(a_{2},\dots,a_{n+1})\longmapsto[X+\sum_{i=2}^{n+1}a_{i}X^{i}]

is bijective. This shows that the measure
\bar{\mu}_{n}
on
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})
deduced from
\mu_{n}
satisfies:

\bar{\mu}_{n}(D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z}))=1.

The measure
\bar{\mu}_{n}
is the unique probability measure on the compact space
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})
invariant under the left action of
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
.

The group:

D(\mathbb{R})\simeq\varprojlim_{n}D_{n}(\mathbb{R}),

as the projective limit of a countable projective system of Lie groups, is a Polish topological group. This topology coincide with the topology induces by the natural topology of Fréchet space on
\mathbb{R}[[X]]\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}.
The topological group
D(\mathbb{R})
contains:

D(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\varprojlim_{n}D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})

as a closed subgroup, and the quotient space:

D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})\simeq\varprojlim_{n}D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})

is compact. We shall denote by
\bar{\mu}
the unique probability measure on the compact space
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
whose direct image by the projection:

\bar{p}_{n}:D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z})

coincides with
\bar{\mu}_{n}
for every
n\geq 1
. The measure
\bar{\mu}
is easily seen to be the unique probability measure on
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
that is invariant under the left action of
D(\mathbb{R})
. This notably implies that the mesure
\bar{\mu}(V)
of any non-empty open subset
V
of
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
is positive.

We shall say that a Borel subset
B
of
D(\mathbb{R})
, invariant under the right action of
D(\mathbb{Z})

has full measure in
D(\mathbb{R})
when:

\bar{\mu}(q(B))=1,

where
q:D(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
denotes the quotient map.

For every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}^{\ast},
we shall denote:

[\lambda]:=\lambda X\quad(\in\mathbb{R}[[X]]).

The map
(g\mapsto[\lambda]\circ g)

establishes a bijection from
D(\mathbb{R})
onto
\lambda X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]].

Our result concerning the generic triviality of the algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi))
in the pseudoconcave case reads as follows:

Theorem 6.4.4
.

For every
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that
|\lambda|>1,
the set:

{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}:=\left\{g\in D(\mathbb{R})\mid{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),[\lambda]\circ g))=\mathbb{Z}\right\}

is a
G_{\delta}
subset, invariant under the right action of
D(\mathbb{Z}),
dense and of full measure in
D(\mathbb{R})
.

In other words, when
|\lambda|>1,
the algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
contains only constant functions for “almost all” choice of
\psi
with
\psi^{\prime}(0)=\lambda
, both in the sense of measure theory and of Baire category.

The proof of Theorem
6.4.4
is the object of the next section. The techniques used in this proof play no role in the next chapters, and this section could be skipped at first reading.

6.5.
Proof of the generic triviality of
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi))
in the pseudo-concave case

6.5.1.

In order to prove Theorem
6.4.4
, we need to introduce some further notation.

For every commutative ring
A
, we define a left action of the group
D(A)
on the algebra
A[[X]]
by:

(6.5.1)

g.\varphi:=\varphi\circ g^{-1},

for
g\in D(A)
and
\varphi\in A[[X]]
.
For every
e\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
and
a\in A\setminus\{0\}
, we also define:

O(e,a):=aX^{e}+X^{e+1}A[[X]].

The sets
O(e,a)
are invariant under the action of
D(A),
and we have:

(6.5.2)

XA[[X]]\setminus\{0\}=\coprod_{e\in\mathbb{N}_{>0},a\in A\setminus\{0\}}O(e,a).

Observe that, when
k
is a field of characteristic zero, the action of
D(k)
on
Xk[[X]]\setminus\{0\}
is free, and
the sets
O(e,a)
for
(e,a)\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times k^{\times}
are precisely the orbits of this action; see
6.5.2
(b) below when
k=\mathbb{R}
.

For every
\rho\in\mathbb{R}_{+},
we define a pseudo-norm
\|.\|_{\rho}
on
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
by the formula:

\big\|\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}a_{i}X^{i}\big\|_{\rho}:=\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}|a_{i}|\rho^{i}\quad(\in[0,+\infty]).

Moreover, for every
R\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, we let:

B_{\rho}(R):=\left\{\varphi\in\mathbb{R}[[X]]\mid\|\varphi\|_{\rho}\leq R\right\}.

Observe that a formal series
\varphi\in\mathbb{R}[[X]]
defines a function analytic on some open neighborhood of
\overline{D}(0,|\lambda|^{-1})
if and only if there exists
\rho
in
(|\lambda|^{-1},+\infty)
such that:

\|\varphi\|_{\rho}<+\infty.

For
e
in
\mathbb{N}_{>0},

a
in
\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\},
and
\rho
and
R
in
\mathbb{R}_{+},
let us consider the following subset of
D(\mathbb{R})
:

F(e,a,\rho,R):=\left\{g\in D(\mathbb{R})\mid g.O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})\cap B_{\rho}(R)\neq\emptyset\right\}.

Our previous observation concerning the analyticity of elements of
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
and the decomposition (
6.5.2
) when
A=\mathbb{Z}
imply the following description of the complement of
{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}
:

\displaystyle D(\mathbb{R})\setminus{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\rho>|\lambda|^{-1}}\left\{g\in D(\mathbb{R})\mid\exists\,\alpha\in g(X\mathbb{Z}[[X]]\setminus\{0\}),\|\alpha\|_{\rho}<+\infty\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{\rho>|\lambda|^{-1},R>0}\left\{g\in D(\mathbb{R})\mid g(X\mathbb{Z}[[X]]\setminus\{0\})\cap B_{\rho}(R)\neq\emptyset\right\}

\displaystyle=\bigcup_{(e,a,\rho,R)\in{\mathcal{E}}(\lambda)}F(e,a,\rho,R),

where:

{\mathcal{E}}(\lambda):=\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})\times(|\lambda|^{-1},+\infty)\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\ast}.

In turn, this immediately shows that
D(\mathbb{R})\setminus{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}
may be written as a countable union:

(6.5.3)

D(\mathbb{R})\setminus{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}=\bigcup_{(e,a,\rho,R)\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\lambda)}F(e,a,\rho,R),

where:

{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}(\lambda):=\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})\times\left\{|\lambda|^{-1}+1/i;i\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}\right\}\times\mathbb{N}.

This decomposition of
D(\mathbb{R})\setminus{\mathcal{T}}_{\lambda}
shows that Theorem
6.4.4
is a consequence of the following proposition and of Baire’s theorem:

Proposition 6.5.1
.

(a) For every
(e,a,\rho,R)
in
\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\})\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
,

F(e,a,\rho,R)
is a closed subset of
D(\mathbb{R})
, invariant under the right action of
D(\mathbb{Z}).

(b) If moreover
\rho>1,
then:

\bar{\mu}(q(F(e,a,\rho,R)))=0,

and
F(e,a,\rho,R)
has an empty interior in
D(\mathbb{R})
.

6.5.2.
Proof of Proposition
6.5.1

Proof of (a).
The invariance of
F(e,a,\rho,R)
under the right action of
D(\mathbb{Z})
follows from its definition.

To prove that
F(e,a,\rho,R)
is closed in
D(\mathbb{R}),
observe that the left action of
D(\mathbb{R})
on
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
, defined above by (
6.5.1
), is continuous and that, for every
(\rho,R)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\ast}\times\mathbb{R}_{+},

B_{\rho}(R)
is a compact subset of the Fréchet space
\mathbb{R}[[X]]
.

Let us consider a sequence
(g_{n})
in
F(e,a,\rho,R)
that admits a limit
g
in
D(\mathbb{R})
. There exists a sequence
(\varphi_{n})
in
O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})
such that the formal series

g_{n}.\varphi_{n}:=\varphi_{n}\circ g_{n}^{-1}

belong to
B_{\rho}(R)
. After possibly passing to a subsequence, we may assume that
(\varphi_{n}\circ g_{n}^{-1})
admits a limit
l
in
B_{\rho}(R)
. Then

(\varphi_{n})=((\varphi_{n}\circ g_{n}^{-1})\circ g_{n})

converges to

\varphi:=l\circ g
in

\mathbb{R}[[X]]
. Since
O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})
is closed in
\mathbb{R}[[X]],
this limit
\varphi
belongs to
O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})
. Finally,

g.\varphi:=\varphi\circ g^{-1}=l,

belongs to
g.O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})\cap B_{\rho}(R)
, and therefore
g
belongs to
F(e,a,\rho,R)
.

Proof of (b).
Observe that, for every
(e,a)
in
\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}),
the map:

I_{e,a}:D(\mathbb{R}):=X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]]\longrightarrow O(e,a)(\mathbb{R}),\quad\psi\longmapsto a\psi^{e}

is bijective. This already implies that the action by composition of
D(\mathbb{R})
on
O(e,a)(\mathbb{R})
is free and transitive.

By truncation, for every
n\in\mathbb{N},
the action of
D(\mathbb{R})
on
O(e,a)(\mathbb{R})
defines an action of
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
on

O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R}):=\left(aX^{e}+X^{e+1}\mathbb{R}[[X]]\right)\!/(X^{n+e+1}).

Moreover, by truncation, for every
n\in\mathbb{N},
the map
I_{e,a}
induces a bijection:

I^{n}_{e,a}:D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R}),

which is clearly compatible with the action of
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by composition on
O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, which is also free and transitive.

We shall denote by
\mu_{n}^{e,a}
the measure on
O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
image of the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
by the diffeomorphism:

\iota_{n}^{e,a}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R}),\quad(a_{e+1},\dots,a_{e+n})\longmapsto aX^{e}+\sum_{i=e+1}^{e+n}a_{i}X^{i}\mod X^{n+e+1}.

Lemma 6.5.2
.

For
every
(e,a)
in
\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\})
and every
\varphi\in O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
, the image of the Haar measure
\mu_{n}
on
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by the diffeomorphism

\bullet.\varphi:D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R}),\quad g\longmapsto g.\varphi

is the measure
(e|a|)^{-n}\mu_{n}^{e,a}
.

Proof.

Since
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
is unimodular, this is equivalent to the fact that the image of
\mu_{n}
by the diffeomorphism:

\bullet^{-1}.\varphi:D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R}),\quad g\longmapsto\varphi\circ g

is the measure
(e|a|)^{-n}\mu_{n}^{e,a}
. In turn, this is equivalent to the fact that the direct image by the diffeomorphism:

J(e,a,n,\varphi):=(\iota_{n}^{e,a})^{-1}\circ(\bullet^{-1}.\varphi)\circ\iota_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{n}

of the
n
-dimensional Lebesgue measure is
(e|a|)^{-n}
times the
n
-dimensional Lebesgue measure. To establish this, we shall show that the Jacobian of
J(e,a,n,\varphi)
, namely
\det DJ(e,a,n,\varphi)
, is constant on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and satisfies:

(6.5.4)

\det DJ(e,a,n,\varphi)=(ea)^{n}.

According to the change-of-variables formula for the Lebesgue measure, this will complete the proof.

To compute the differential of the diffeomorphism
\bullet^{-1}.\varphi
, we may identify
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
and
O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
to
X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}
and to
aX^{e}+X^{e+1}\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}
respectively, were
\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}
denotes the
\mathbb{R}
-vector space of polynomial of degree
<n
. Then the differential of
\bullet^{-1}.\varphi
at some point
g
of
D_{n}(\mathbb{R})
maps an element
\delta g
in
X^{2}\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}
to:

D(\bullet^{-1}.\varphi)(g)\delta g=(\varphi^{\prime}\circ g)\,\delta g\in\left(X^{e+1}\mathbb{R}[[X]]\right)/(X^{n+e+1})\simeq X^{e+1}\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}.

This shows that, after identifying
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathbb{R}[X]_{<n}
by means of the standard basis:

(1,X,\dots,X^{n-1}),

the differential
DJ(e,a,n,\varphi)
at the point
\iota_{n}^{-1}(g)
is given by the mutiplication:

\mathbb{R}[[X]]_{<n}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}[[X]]_{<n},\quad P\longmapsto\lfloor X^{-e+1}(\varphi^{\prime}\circ g)P\rfloor,

where
\lfloor X^{-e+1}(\varphi^{\prime}\circ g)P\rfloor
denotes the unique representative in
\mathbb{R}[[X]]_{<n}
of the class

\mod X^{n}
of
\mathbb{R}[[X]]_{<n}
.
Since
X^{-e+1}(\varphi^{\prime}\circ g)
is an element of

ae+X\mathbb{R}[[X]],

the determinant of this map is
(ea)^{n}
.
∎

For every
(e,a)
in
\mathbb{N}_{>0}\times(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\{0\}),
we may introduce the following subset of
O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})
:

\Delta(e,a):=\Big\{aX^{e}+\sum_{i\geq e+1}a_{i}X^{i}\in\mathbb{Z}[[X]]\mid\forall i\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq e+1},0\leq a_{i}<e|a|\Big\}.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we shall also denote by
\Delta(e,a)_{n}
the image of
\Delta(e,a)
in
O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by the truncation morphism. In other words:

\Delta(e,a)_{n}:=\Big\{\Big[aX^{e}+\sum_{i=e+1}^{e+n}a_{i}X^{i}\Big]\in\mathbb{Z}[[X]]/(X^{n+e+1})\mid\forall i\in\{e+1,\dots,e+n\},0\leq a_{i}<e|a|\Big\}.

Lemma 6.5.3
.

With the previous notation, we have:

D(\mathbb{Z}).\Delta(e,a):=\bigcup_{\gamma\in D(\mathbb{Z})}\gamma.\Delta(a,e)=O(e,a).

Proof.

For every element:

\varphi:=aX^{e}+\sum_{i\geq e+1}a_{i}X^{i}

in
O(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})
and for every integer
k\geq 2
and every
b\in\mathbb{Z},
the image of
\varphi
under the action of the element

X+bX^{k}
of
D(\mathbb{Z})
is:

\displaystyle(X+bX^{k}).\varphi

\displaystyle:=\varphi\circ(X+bX^{k})^{\circ(-1)}

\displaystyle=\varphi\circ(X-bX^{k})\mod X^{e+k}

\displaystyle=aX^{e}+\sum_{e+1\leq i\leq e+k-2}a_{i}X^{i}+(a_{e+k-1}-aeb)X^{e+k-1}\mod X^{e+k}.

This identity allows one to produce an element
\gamma
in
D(\mathbb{Z})
such that
\varphi
is contained in
\gamma.\Delta(a,e)
by defining
\gamma^{-1}
as the limit of elements of the form

(X+b_{k}X^{k})\circ\dots\circ(X+b_{2}X^{2}),

with
b_{2},\dots,b_{k}
in
\mathbb{Z}
, when
k\geq 2
goes to infinity. ∎

According to Lemma
6.5.3
, we have:

(6.5.5)

q(F(e,a,\rho,R))=q(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)),

where:

\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)):=\left\{g\in D(\mathbb{R})\mid g.\Delta(e,a)(\mathbb{Z})\cap B_{\rho}(R)\neq\emptyset\right\}.

For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we shall denote by:

p_{n}:D(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow D_{n}(\mathbb{R}):=D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/H_{n}(\mathbb{R})

the quotient (or truncation) morphism. It fits into the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{D(\mathbb{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{n}}
\scriptstyle{q\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\bar{p}_{n}}
\textstyle{D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{n}\hskip 8.19447pt}
\textstyle{D_{n}(\mathbb{R})/D_{n}(\mathbb{Z}).}

Therefore the following inequalities hold:

(6.5.6)

\bar{\mu}(q(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)))\leq\bar{\mu}(\bar{p}_{n}^{-1}(\bar{p}_{n}\circ q(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R))))=\bar{\mu}_{n}(\bar{p}_{n}\circ q(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)))\\
=\bar{\mu}_{n}(q_{n}\circ p_{n}(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)))\leq\mu_{n}(p_{n}(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R))).

Moreover the image of
O(e,a)(\mathbb{R})\cap B_{\rho}(R)
in
O(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{R})
by the truncation map is contained in:

\iota_{n}^{e,a}\Big(\prod_{i=e+1}^{e+n}[-R\rho^{-i},R\rho^{-i}]\Big).

Consequently we have:

p_{n}(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R))\subseteq\bigcup_{\varphi\in\Delta(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{Z})}\Big\{g\in D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\mid g.\varphi\in\iota_{n}^{e,a}(\prod_{i=e+1}^{e+n}[-R\rho^{-i},R\rho^{-i}])\Big\},

and therefore:

\mu_{n}(p_{n}(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)))\leq\sum_{\varphi\in\Delta(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{Z})}\mu_{n}\Big(\big\{g\in D_{n}(\mathbb{R})\mid g.\varphi\in\iota_{n}^{e,a}(\prod_{i=e+1}^{e+n}[-R\rho^{-i},R\rho^{-i}])\Big\}\Big).

Using Lemma
6.5.2
, we finally obtain:

(6.5.7)

\begin{split}\mu_{n}(p_{n}(\widetilde{F}(e,a,\rho,R)))&\leq\big|\Delta(e,a)_{n}(\mathbb{Z})\big|\,\,(e|a|)^{-n}\,\prod_{i=e+1}^{e+n}(2R\rho^{-i})\\
&\quad\quad=(2R)^{n}\rho^{-\sum_{i=e+1}^{e+n}i}=(2R)^{n}\rho^{-(n+2e+2)(n-1)/2}.\end{split}

If
\rho>1,
the right-hand side of (
6.5.7
) goes to
0
when
n
goes to infinity. Together with (
6.5.5
) and (
6.5.6
), this establishes the vanishing of
\bar{\mu}(q(F(e,a,\rho,R)))
when
\rho>1
. In turn this vanishing implies that the interior
\mu(q(F(e,a,\rho,R)))
in
D(\mathbb{R})/D(\mathbb{Z})
is empty, as observed above in
6.4.3
.

Chapter 7
Maps from formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces to arithmetic schemes

In this chapter, we define morphisms from a smooth f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to an arithmetic scheme
1
1

1

that is, a separated scheme of finite type over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
.

X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, for
K
some number field, and we discuss some basic constructions involving them.

When
X
is a normal arithmetic surface, we investigate the relations of the rudimentary arithmetic intersection theory on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
introduced in Section
6.2
with the more classical arithmetic intersection theory on quasi-projective arithmetic surfaces, as presented in Chapters
3
and
4
.
These relations involve the Archimedean overflow
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha:(V,P)\rightarrow N)
studied in Chapter
5
and its counterpart “at finite places”
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
.

Finally we extend these results to the situation where the morphisms from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to
X
are replaced by suitably defined “meromorphic maps.”

We denote by
K
a number field, and by
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
its ring of integers.

7.1.
Morphisms from formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces to
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes

7.1.1.
Definitions and basic properties

If
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},O_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
denotes a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
as defined in
6.1.1
, and if
X
is a separated scheme of finite type over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, we may define a
morphism

f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

over

{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
as a pair:

f:=(\widehat{f},(f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

where:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

is a morphism of formal schemes over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
and, for every complex embedding
\sigma
of
K
,

f_{\sigma}:V_{\sigma}\longrightarrow X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})

is a complex analytic map.

These data are moreover assumed to be compatible with the gluing data
(\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
. Namely, for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
the morphisms from the smooth formal curve
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}
to the complex scheme
X_{\sigma}
deduced from
\widehat{f}
(by the base change
\sigma:{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
) and from
f_{\sigma}
(by considering its “formal germ” at
P_{\sigma}
) are required to coincide; see
[
Bos20
, 10.6.3]
.

As a consequence, these data are compatible with complex conjugation.

We shall use the notation:

f^{\rm an}:V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow X(\mathbb{C})

for the complex analytic map from:

V^{+}_{\mathbb{C}}:=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}V^{+}_{\sigma}

to:

X(\mathbb{C})=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})

defined by the maps
f_{\sigma}
.

If
E
(resp.
{\overline{E}}:=(E,\|.\|)
) is a vector bundle over
X
(resp. a Hermitian vector bundle over
X
, supposed to have a reduced generic fiber
X_{K}
), we may form its pull-back
f^{\ast}E
(resp.
f^{\ast}{\overline{E}}
), defined by the “formal” pull-back
\widehat{f}^{\ast}E
on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
and the “complex analytic” pull-back
f_{\sigma}^{\ast}E_{\sigma}
(resp.
f_{\sigma}^{\ast}{\overline{E}}_{\sigma}
) on
V_{\sigma}^{+}
, and some canonical gluing data; see
[
Bos20
, 10.6.3 ]
.

To every morphism
f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X
as in
7.1.1
, we may attach the Zariski closures of the images of the morphisms of ringed spaces:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X,\quad\widehat{f}_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\longrightarrow X_{K},\quad\widehat{f}_{\sigma}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}\longrightarrow X_{\sigma},\quad\mbox{and}\quad f_{\sigma}:V_{\sigma}^{+}\longrightarrow X_{\sigma},

which we shall denote by
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{K}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{\sigma}}
and
\overline{\mathrm{im}f_{\sigma}}
. Each of these is defined as the smallest closed subscheme of the range of the morphism through which the morphism factors; see
[
Bos20
, 10.6.4]
. These schemes are integral. Moreover,
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}
is flat over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{K}}
is geometrically irreducible over
K
, and the following relations hold:

\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{K}}=\big(\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}\big)_{K}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\overline{\mathrm{im}f_{\sigma}}=\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{\sigma}}=\big(\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}\big)_{\sigma}.

We say that
f
is a
constant
morphism when the following equivalent conditions are satisfied:

\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}=1,\quad\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{K}}=0,\quad\mbox{or}\quad\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}f_{\sigma}}=0.

This holds precisely when
f
factors through the morphism:

\pi_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},

or equivalently, when
f
may written as
f=Q\circ\pi_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}
for some
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point
Q
of
X
. In concrete terms, this holds if and only if the morphism:

\widehat{f}_{K}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\simeq\mathrm{Spf}K[[T]]\longrightarrow X_{K}

is constant in the obvious sense.

The
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}
may be identified with the elements of the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, and the morphism:

f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}

defined by an element
f
of
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is constant if and only if
f
belongs to the subring
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
of
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}).

Finally, we say that the image of an
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphism:

f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

is
algebraic
when the following equivalent conditions hold:

\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}}\leq 2,\quad\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{f}_{K}}\leq 1,\quad\mbox{or}\quad\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}f_{\sigma}}\leq 1.

7.1.2.
Morphisms and sections of vector bundles

As in
7.1.1
, let us consider a scheme
X
separated and of finite type over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. Let us also assume that
X
is reduced and that the structure morphism
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
is proper and flat.

For every Hermitian vector bundle
\overline{E}=(E,\|.\|)
over
X
— defined by a vector bundle
E
on
X
and a continuous metric
\|.\|
on the complex analytic vector bundle
E^{\mathrm{an}}_{\mathbb{C}}
on:

X(\mathbb{C})=\coprod_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})

— the direct image
\pi_{X*}E
is a vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. Moreover, for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, the complex vector space:

(\pi_{X*}E)_{\sigma}\simeq\Gamma(X_{\sigma},E_{\sigma})

may be endowed with the norm
\|.\|_{\infty,\sigma}
defined by:

\|s\|_{\infty,\sigma}:=\sup_{x\in X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})}\|s(x)\|.

In general, the norm
\|.\|_{\infty,\sigma}
is not a Hermitian norm. However we may consider the
John norm

\|.\|_{J,\sigma}
on
\Gamma(X_{\sigma},E_{\sigma})
attached to
\|.\|_{\infty,\sigma}
, namely, the smallest Hermitian norm such that

\|.\|_{\infty,\sigma}\leq\|.\|_{J,\sigma};

see
[
Bos20
, Appendix F]
. This construction defines a Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\pi^{J}_{X\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=(\pi_{X*}E,(\|.\|_{J,\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}).

The following proposition is a straightforward consequence of the definitions:

Proposition 7.1.1

(compare to
[
Bos20
, 10.8.1]
)
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, let
\mu
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
positive volume form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, invariant under complex conjugation, and let:

f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

be a morphism over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

For every Hermitian vector bundle
\overline{E}
over
X
,
the pull-back of sections of
E
by
f
defines a morphism:

\varphi_{E}:\pi^{J}_{X\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}\longrightarrow\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}f^{*}\overline{E}

of (pro-)Hermitian vector bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. If moreover:

\int_{V_{\sigma}}\mu\leq 1

for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, then the
Archimedean norms of
\varphi_{E}
are bounded above by
1
.

If
\alpha:X\rightarrow Y
is morphism between two separated
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes of finite type, and if

f:=(\widehat{f},(f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

is a morphism over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
from a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to
X
, then we may form the composition
\alpha\circ f
from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to
Y
:

\alpha\circ f:=(\alpha\circ\widehat{f},(\alpha_{\sigma}\circ f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow Y.

This construction satisfies obvious compatibilities with the pull-back of vector bundle, and with the pull-back of sections of vector bundles discussed in
7.1.2
.

7.2.
Morphisms to arithmetic surfaces, arithmetic intersection numbers, and overflow

In this section, we consider
a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
as defined in
6.1.1
above, an integral normal arithmetic surface:

\pi_{X}:X\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},

and a morphism over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\alpha:=(\widehat{\alpha},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X,

as defined in
7.1.1
.

We shall denote by:

P:{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

the canonical section of the structural morphism of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
:

\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},

and by:

Q:=\widehat{\alpha}\circ P:{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}\longrightarrow X

the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point of
X
defined as the image of
P
by
\widehat{\alpha}
. The existence of this
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point guarantees that
X_{K}
is geometrically irreducible over
K
, and that the fibers of
\pi_{X}
are geometrically connected when moreover
X
is projective.

We shall also assume that
\alpha
is non-constant, and we shall denote by
e(\alpha)
the ramification index of the morphism of smooth (formal) curves over
K
:

\widehat{\alpha}_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\longrightarrow X_{K}.

7.2.1.
The map
\alpha_{\ast}:{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\rightarrow{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}(X)

To any Arakelov divisor
(D,g)=(D,(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),
we may attach its
direct image by the morphism

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X
, defined as:

\alpha_{\ast}(D,g):=({\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}D,(\alpha_{\sigma\ast}g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}).

It is straightforward that it is an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}_{c}^{1}(X)
, and that this construction attaches a morphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules:

\alpha_{\ast}:{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\longrightarrow{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}(X)

to any non-constant morphism
\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

Observe that the “arithmetic part” of this construction — namely the map
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}
that maps a divisor supported by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
to its direct image in
X
— is nothing more than the map that sends a multiple
nP
of the section
P
, for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, to the multiple
nQ
of the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point

Q:=\alpha^{\rm an}(P)

of
X
image of
P
by
\alpha^{\rm an}
.

This construction satisfies the following compatibility with the direct image functoriality for the Arakelov divisors on quasi-projective arithmetic surfaces:

Proposition 7.2.1
.

For every
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphisms
f:X\rightarrow X^{\prime},

from
X
to an integral normal arithmetic surface
X^{\prime}
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
and every Arakelov divisor
(D,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, the following equality holds in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}(X^{\prime})
:

(7.2.1)

(f\circ\alpha)_{\ast}(D,g)=f_{\ast}\alpha_{\ast}(D,g).

The arithmetic intersection theory on the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
introduced in Subsection
6.2.3
and the “classical” arithmetic intersection theory on the normal quasi-projective arithmetic surface
X
developed in Chapters
3
and
4
are related by the following projection formula:

Proposition 7.2.2
.

Let
\overline{L}:=(L,(\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
be a Hermitian line bundle on
X
, defined by Hermitian metrics
\|.\|_{\sigma})
of regularity
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
. For every Arakelov divisor
(D,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}_{c}^{1}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),
the following equality of arithmetic intersection numbers holds:

(7.2.2)

\alpha^{*}{\overline{L}}\cdot(P,g)={\overline{L}}\cdot\alpha_{*}(P,g).

Propositions
7.2.1
and
7.2.2
are straightforward consequences of the definitions and of the basic properties of direct images of (Green) functions.

7.2.2.
The invariant
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)

In this paragraph, we assume that
X
is regular. Then
Q:=\widehat{\alpha}(P)
is an effective Cartier divisor in
X
, and its inverse image by
\widehat{\alpha}
defines an effective (Cartier) divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
:

\widehat{\alpha}^{\ast}(Q)=\widehat{\alpha}^{\ast}(\widehat{\alpha}(P)).

The divisor
P
appears with multiplicity
e(\alpha)
in this divisor, and we may write:

(7.2.3)

\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(Q)=e(\alpha)P+R,

where
R
is an effective Cartier divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
that intersects
P
properly.

In particular, the intersection
R\cdot P
is a well-defined effective
0
-cycle with support on the arithmetic curve
P
; namely:

(7.2.4)

R\cdot P:=\sum_{x\in P_{0}}n_{x}x,

where
P_{0}
denotes the set of closed points of the scheme
P
and
n_{x}
the length of the
\mathcal{O}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},x}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{R\cap P}
. The multiplicity
n_{x}
is positive if and only if the closed point
x
belongs to the support
|R|
of
R
.

The
0
-cycle
R\cdot P
has a well-defined arithmetic degree:

(7.2.5)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P=\sum_{x\in P_{0}}n_{x}\log|\kappa(x)|=\log|\mathcal{O}(R\cap P)|,

where
\kappa(x)
denotes the residue field of
x
and
\mathcal{O}(R\cap P)
is the ring of regular functions on the
0
-dimensional subscheme
R\cap P
of
P
. The arithmetic degree
\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P
is non-negative and vanishes if and only if
R=0
.

Example 7.2.3
.

Assume that
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}=\mathrm{Spf\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}[[T]]
and
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}
. Then
\widehat{\alpha}
identifies with a formal series in
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}[[T]]
, which may be written:

\widehat{\alpha}=a_{0}+\sum_{i\geq e}a_{i}T^{i}

with
e\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
and
a_{e}\neq 0.
Then
Q
is the divisor
(X-a_{0}=0)
in
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}
, the ramification degree of
\widehat{\alpha}_{K}
is
e
, and
R
is the divisor of:

T^{-e}(\widehat{\alpha}-a_{0})=\sum_{k\geq 0}a_{k+e}T^{k}

in
\mathrm{Spf\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}[[T]]
.
The cycle
R\cdot P
coincides with the divisor of
a_{e}
in
P={\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and therefore:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P=\log|N_{K/\mathbb{Q}}\,a_{e}|.

The
0
-cycle
R\cdot P
, or equivalently the arithmetic degree
\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P
, vanishes if and only if
a_{e}
is a unit in
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

When the arithmetic surface
X
is no longer assumed to be regular, but only normal, the previous construction still makes sense with the following modifications.

The divisor
Q
in
X
may not be a Cartier divisor, but it is always
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier; see
0.6.3
. If
N
denotes a positive integer such that
NQ
is a Cartier divisor in
X,
then we may define
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\ast}(Q)
as the effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor:

\widehat{\alpha}^{\ast}(Q):=\frac{1}{N}\widehat{\alpha}^{\ast}(NQ).

Then we may define
R
as an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
by (
7.2.3
), and
R\cdot P
as an effective
0
-cycle with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients supported by
P
. We may finally define
\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P
by (
7.2.4
) and by the first equality in (
7.2.5
).

Definition 7.2.4
.

With the above notation, we define:

(7.2.6)

{\mathrm{Ex}}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big):=\widehat{\rm deg}\,R\cdot P.

In other words, we have:

(7.2.7)

{\mathrm{Ex}}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)=\widehat{\rm deg}\,\big(\widehat{\alpha}^{\ast}(\widehat{\alpha}(P))-e(\alpha)P\big)\cdot P.

The invariant
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
constitutes an arithmetic counterpart of the Archimedean overflow invariant
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha:V\rightarrow X)
introduced in Chapter
5
; compare for instance (
7.2.7
) and the expression (
5.2.3
) for
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha:V\rightarrow X)

in terms of
\ast
-product.

The invariant
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
is a non-negative real number in
\mathbb{Q}^{\ast}_{+}\log\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. However, there are instances where we may guarantee that the invariant
\mathrm{Ex}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
is of the form:

\mathrm{Ex}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)=\log n

for some positive integer
n
even when
X
is not assumed to be regular. This is an immediate consequence of the following statement.

Proposition 7.2.5
.

Assume that
\widehat{\alpha}
is quasi-finite, namely, that the fibers of
\widehat{\alpha}
over closed points of
X
are finite. Let
D
be an effective Weil divisor on
X
and let
N
be a positive integer such that
ND
is Cartier.
Then there exists a positive integer
n
such that

N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)\cdot P=\log n.

In particular, if
N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)\cdot P\neq 0,
then
N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)\cdot P\geq\log 2.

To prove Proposition
7.2.5
, it is enough to show that the effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
N^{-1}\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)
is actually a divisor, namely, that it has integral coefficients. In turn, this is equivalent to showing that if
E
is an irreducible Cartier divisor on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, that appears as a component of the effective Cartier divisor
\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)
, then the multiplicity of
E
in
\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)
is a multiple of
N
. If
E=P,
the result is clear, so we may assume that
E
is not
P
.

In order to simplify the argument we will only prove that the multiplicity of
E
in
\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(NE)
is larger than
N
. This still proves that the intersection number
N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)\cdot P
is at least equal to
\log 2
when it is nonzero, which is the only part of the statement that we will use in this monograph – see
9.1.3
below. We leave the derivation of the more precise statement to the interested reader.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{Z}
be the preimage of
D
in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
and let
D^{\prime}
be the associated effective Cartier divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
obtained by discarding the lower-dimensional components and the embedded points of
\widehat{Z}
. By construction, the schematic image of
D^{\prime}
is contained in
D
and
E
is a component of
D^{\prime}
. The schematic image of
E
in
X
is purely one-dimensional in
X
since
\widehat{\alpha}
is quasi-finite. In particular, the schematic image of
NE
is contained in
ND
, so that
NE
is a component of
\widehat{\alpha}^{*}(ND)
.
∎

We shall call
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
the
overflow
or
excess
of the morphism
\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X
. It clearly depends only on the morphism from
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
to the formal completion
\widehat{X}_{Q}
of
X
along its section
Q=\widehat{\alpha}(P)
defined by
\widehat{\alpha}
, and accordingly we will also denote it by
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow\widehat{X}_{Q}).

7.2.3.
The self-intersections of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)
and
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})

Let
g:=(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
be a family of Green functions for the points
P_{\sigma}
in the Riemann surfaces
V^{+}_{\sigma}
such that
(P,g)
is
an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
. In other words, for every field embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},

g_{\sigma}
is a Green function with
{\mathcal{C}}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
regularity that vanishes on
V^{+}_{\sigma}\setminus\mathring{V}_{\sigma}
, and the family
(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
is invariant under complex conjugation.

To each of the Green functions
g_{\sigma}
is associated a capacitary metric
\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}}
on the complex line :

T_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})_{\sigma},

as defined in
3.2.1
, and we may consider the Hermitian line bundle

{\overline{N}}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}:=(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

over
P\simeq|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|.

The following proposition expresses the self-intersection of the direct image
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}(X)
in terms of the Arakelov degree of the metrized normal bundle
{\overline{N}}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
and of the invariants
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
and
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma})
attached to the formal and Archimedean components
{\widehat{\alpha}}
and
\alpha_{\sigma}
of the morphism
\alpha
.

Proposition 7.2.6
.

With the notation above, the following equality holds:

(7.2.8)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}+\mathrm{Ex}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma}).

Proposition
7.2.6
will be a consequence of the following lemma:

Lemma 7.2.7
.

Let
Q:={\widehat{\alpha}}(P)
be the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point of
X
image of
P
by
{\widehat{\alpha}}
, and let
N
be a positive integer such the divisor
NQ
in
X
is Cartier. Then the following equality holds:

(7.2.9)

N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}+\mathrm{Ex}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{\sigma}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma*}(Q_{\sigma})-eP_{\sigma}}.

Proof of Lemma
7.2.7
.

With the notation introduced in
7.2.2
, we have the equality of Cartier divisors in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
:

(7.2.10)

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\ast}(NQ)=e(\alpha)NP+NR,

and therefore an isomorphism of line bundles over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
:

(7.2.11)

I:{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(NQ)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(e(\alpha)NP)\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(NR).

Recall that
NR
is an effective Cartier divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
such that
|NR|\cap|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
is a finite set of closed points. In particular
P\cap NR
is a well-defined effective divisor in
P
.

By restriction to the section
P,
the isomorphism
I
this defines an isomorphism of line bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

(7.2.12)

I_{\mid P}:Q^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(NQ)=P^{\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(NQ)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}P^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\big(e(\alpha)NP\big)\otimes P^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}(NR)\\
=P^{\ast}N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\otimes e(\alpha)N}\otimes P^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{P}(P\cap NR).

For every field embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
the isomorphism
I_{\mid P}
induces an isomorphism of complex lines:

I_{\mid P,\sigma}:\big(T_{Q_{\sigma}}X_{\sigma}\big)^{\otimes N}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X_{\sigma}}(NQ_{\sigma})_{\mid Q_{\sigma}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(T_{Q_{\sigma}}X_{\sigma})^{\otimes e(\alpha)N}.

Its norm, with respect to the Hermitian metrics
\|.\|_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma,*}g_{\sigma}}^{\otimes N}
and
(\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\sigma}})^{\otimes e(\alpha)N}
is readily seen to be:

\|I_{\mid P,\sigma}\|=\exp\Big(-N\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{\sigma}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma,*}(Q_{\sigma})-eP_{\sigma}}\Big).

This implies the following relation between Arakelov degrees:

\displaystyle\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})^{\otimes e(\alpha)N}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,(NR)\cdot P-\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\log\|I_{\mid P,\sigma}\|

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})^{\otimes e(\alpha)N}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,(NR)\cdot P+N\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{\sigma}\delta_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma,*}(Q_{\sigma})-eP_{\sigma}}.

Using the additivity of the map:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,:\overline{{\rm Pic}}({\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}

and dividing by
N
, this becomes the equality (
7.2.9
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.2.6
.

According to the definition (
3.3.4
) of the Arakelov intersection pairing on
X
, we have:

\displaystyle\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g)

\displaystyle=N^{-1}(NQ,N\alpha_{\ast}^{\rm an}g)\cdot(Q,\alpha_{\ast}^{\rm an}g))

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)+N^{-1}\int_{X(\mathbb{C})}\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g\,\,\omega(N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})}\alpha_{\sigma\ast}g_{\sigma}\,\alpha_{\sigma\ast}\omega(g_{\sigma}).

Together with the equality (
7.2.9
) and the definition (
5.1.1
) of the invariants
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma})
, this establishes (
7.2.8
).
∎

When
g=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}},
that is when the Green functions
g_{\sigma}
are the equilibrium potentials

g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}
associated to the points
P_{\sigma}
of the compact Riemann surface with boundary
V_{\sigma}
, then the Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{N}}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
is the Hermitian line bundle
\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
introduced in (
6.2.3
), and the invariant
\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma})
is the overflow
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})
. Consequently, applied to
g=g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}},
Proposition
7.2.6
becomes the following result, which will play a key role in the remainder of this memoir.

Corollary 7.2.8
.

For every non-constant morphism over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
,

\alpha:=(\widehat{\alpha},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X,

from a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to an integral normal arithmetic surface
X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, the following equality holds:

(7.2.13)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}+\mathrm{Ex}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Ex}\big(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})\big).

In turn, the non-negativity of the overflow invariants implies:

Corollary 7.2.9
.

With the notation above, we have:

(7.2.14)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\geq e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}.

7.2.4.
Self-intersection of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
and Green function for the diagonal

We may combine Corollary
7.2.8

and the expression in Theorem
5.4.1
for the archimedean overflow in terms of a Green function of the diagonal. To formulate the resulting expression for the self-intersection of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
, we need to introduce some notation.

For every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
we assume that the connected Riemann surface
X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})
is endowed with a real
2
-form
\beta_{\sigma}
of class
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
, and that:

g_{X_{\sigma}}:X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})\times X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty]

is a Green function for the diagonal of
X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})
associated to
\beta_{\sigma},
as defined in
5.3.1
. We assume that the family
(g_{X_{\sigma}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}},
and therefore the family
(\beta_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
is invariant under complex conjugation.

Recall that we may attach the capacitary metric
\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}}
on the complex tangent bundle
T_{X_{\sigma}}

to the Green function
g_{\sigma}
; see
5.3.1
. The family of Hermitian metrics
(\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
is clearly invariant under complex conjugation.

We shall also assume that the image of
the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point
Q:={\widehat{\alpha}}(P)
of
X
lies in the regular locus of

X
. Then the normal bundle
N_{Q}X
is a well-defined line bundle over
Q
, and equipped with the capacitary metrics
\|.\|_{g_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}}
on the complex lines:

(N_{Q}X)_{\sigma}\simeq T_{X_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma}},

it defines a Hermitian line bundle over
Q
:

{\overline{N}}_{Q}^{\mathrm{cap}}X:=\big(N_{Q}X,(\|.\|_{g_{\sigma},Q_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big).

Proposition 7.2.10
.

With the notation above, the following formula holds:

(7.2.15)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{Q}^{\mathrm{cap}}X\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Big(2\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}\,\alpha_{\sigma}^{*}\beta_{\sigma}-\int_{(\partial V_{\sigma})^{2}}g_{X_{\sigma}}(\alpha_{\sigma}(z_{1}),\alpha_{\sigma}(z_{2}))\,d\mu_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}(z_{1})\,d\mu_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}(z_{2})\Big).

Observe that the image of the section
Q
actually lies in the open subscheme
X_{\rm sm}
of
X
where the structure morphisme
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
is smooth. Endowed with the Hermitian metrics
\big(\|.\|^{\rm cap}_{g_{\sigma}}\big)_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
on the Riemann surfaces
X_{{\rm sm},\sigma}(\mathbb{C})=X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C}),
the relative tangent bundle
T_{\pi_{X}}
over
X_{\rm sm}
defines a Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}
over
X_{\rm sm}
. The Hermitian line bundle
Q^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{Q}^{\mathrm{cap}}X
may be identified with
Q^{\ast}{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}
, and its Arakelov degree coincides with the height of
Q
with respect to
{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}
:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{Q}^{\mathrm{cap}}X=\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}=:0pt_{{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}}(Q).

Proof.

Let us write
e:=e(\alpha)
. The
e
-jet
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}^{[e]}(P_{K})
of
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
at
P_{K}
defines an isomorphism of
K
-lines:

{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}^{[e]}:\big(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\big)_{K}^{\otimes e}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{Q_{K}}X_{K}.

For every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
, by means of the identification:

(T\iota_{\sigma})^{\otimes e}:\big(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})_{\sigma}^{\otimes e}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(T_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma}\big)^{\otimes e},

the
base change of
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}^{[e]}
by
\sigma
becomes the isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-lines:

\alpha_{\sigma}^{[e]}(P_{\sigma}):\big(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})_{\sigma}^{\otimes e}{\simeq}\big(T_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma}\big)^{\otimes e}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{Q_{\sigma}}X_{\sigma},

already considered, in a more general context, in Subsection
5.4.1
. Its norm with respect to the metric
\|.\|_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}\otimes e}
on
\big(T_{P_{\sigma}}V_{\sigma}\big)^{\otimes e}
and
\|.\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{X_{\sigma}}}
on
T_{Q_{\sigma}}X_{\sigma}
will be denoted by
\|\alpha_{\sigma}^{[e]}(P_{\sigma})\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}
.
With this notation, according to Theorem
5.4.1
, the following equality holds for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
:

(7.2.16)

\mathrm{Ex}\big(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma}\big)=2\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}\,\alpha_{\sigma}^{*}\beta_{\sigma}\\
-\int_{(\partial V_{\sigma})^{2}}g_{X_{\sigma}}(\alpha_{\sigma}(z_{1}),\alpha_{\sigma}(z_{2}))\,d\mu_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}(z_{1})\,d\mu_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}(z_{2})-\log\|\alpha_{\sigma}^{[e]}(P_{\sigma})\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}.

Observe that the isomorphism
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}^{[e]}
is the inverse of the restriction to
{\rm Spec\,}K
of the isomorphism
I_{\mid P}
in the proof of Lemma
7.2.7
in the special case
N=1
. Therefore
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}^{[e]}
extends to an isomorphism of line bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{[e]}:P^{\ast}N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\otimes e}\otimes P^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{P}(P\cap R)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q^{\ast}N_{Q}X,

where
R
is defined as in
7.2.2
. This implies the following relation between Arakelov degrees:

(7.2.17)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{N}_{Q}X=e\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P\cdot R-\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\log\|\alpha_{\sigma}^{[e]}(P_{\sigma})\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{e}.

The equality (
7.2.15
) follows from
the expression (
7.2.13
) for the self-intersection of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
, combined with (
7.2.16
), (
7.2.17
), and the definition (
7.2.6
) of
{\mathrm{Ex}}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)
.
∎

Let
\psi
a formal series in:

G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R}):=\mathbb{R}^{\ast}X+X^{2}\mathbb{R}[[X]],

and let:

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}

be a morphism from the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)
over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
attached to
\psi
defined in
6.4.1
.

Equivalently,
\alpha
is an element of the ring
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)
of regular functions over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)
. It is defined as a pair:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},\alpha^{{\rm an}}),

where
{\widehat{\alpha}}
is a morphism from
{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
to
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
— that is an element of
\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
— and
\alpha^{{\rm an}}
is an analytic function:

\alpha^{{\rm an}}:{\overline{D}}(0,1)^{+}\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{C})=\mathbb{C},

that satisfy the gluing relation:

(7.2.18)

{\widehat{\alpha}}=\alpha^{\rm an}\circ\psi,

when
{\widehat{\alpha}},

\alpha^{\rm an}
, and
\psi
are seen as formal series in
\mathbb{C}[[X]]
. The morphism
\alpha
is non-constant if and only if
\alpha^{\rm an}
belongs to
\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\setminus\mathbb{Z}
, or equivalently if and only if
\alpha^{{\rm an}}
is not a constant analytic function.

Corollary 7.2.11
.

For every
\psi\in G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R})
, and every non-constant morphism:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},\alpha^{{\rm an}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)\longrightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}},

the following equality holds:

(7.2.19)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=2\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\log\left|\alpha^{\rm an}(e^{2\pi it_{1}})-\alpha^{\rm an}(e^{2\pi it_{2}})\right|\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

Proof.

The function:

g_{\mathbb{C}}:\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow(-\infty,+\infty],\quad(z_{1},z_{2})\longrightarrow\log|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{-1}

is a Green function for the diagonal of
\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{C})
, associated to the
2
-form
\beta=0.
The associated capacitary metric on
T_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{C}}}
satisfies:

\|\partial/\partial z\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{g_{\mathbb{C}}}=1.

Moreover the equilibrium measure
\mu_{{\overline{D}}(0,1),0}
associated to the equilibrium potential:

g_{{\overline{D}}(0,1),0}=(z\mapsto\log^{+}|z|^{-1})

for the origin in the unit disk, is the normalized Haar measure on:

\partial{\overline{D}}(0,1)=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\mid|z|=1\}=U(1).

Using these observations, it is readily seen that, applied to
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}={\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
, to the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)
and to
X=\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
equipped with the Green function
g_{\mathbb{C}}
, the expression (
7.2.15
) for the self-intersection of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
becomes (
7.2.19
).
∎

Corollary
7.2.11
admits a variant, concerning a morphism
\alpha
from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}({\overline{D}}(0,1),\psi)
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}},
obtained by using the Green function
g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
for the diagonal of
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
introduced in
5.3.2
(2) in the expression (
7.2.15
) for the self-intersection of
\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
.
As already observed in
5.4.2
, the first integral in the right-hand side of (
7.2.15
) is the value at 1 of the characteristic function
T_{\alpha}
of Nevanlinna-Ahlfors-Shimizu associated to
\alpha
.

The following formulation of this special instance of Proposition
5.4.2
emphasizes this relation to Nevalinna theory, and facilitates the comparison of our results in Section
8.3
below with the ones in
[
CDT21
]
.

Consider the following data:

•

a formal series
\psi
in
G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R})
;

•

a morphism of (formal) schemes:

{\widehat{\alpha}}:{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}};

•

a meromorphic function, defined on the open disk
D(0,R)
of radius
R\in(0,+\infty]
:

\alpha^{{\rm an}}:D(0,R)\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C});

and assume that
{\widehat{\alpha}}
and
\alpha^{\rm an}
are not constant and satisfy the gluing relation (
7.2.18
).

Let us denote by:

0pt:\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}_{+}

the usual height, defined by:

(7.2.20)

0pt(x_{0}:x_{1}):=\log\left(x_{0}^{2}+x_{1}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

for every pair
(x_{0},x_{1})
of integers that are prime together.

For every
r\in(0,+\infty),
we may consider the following f.-a.​​ surface over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}:={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,r),\psi),

and the morphism:

\alpha_{r}:=\big({\widehat{\alpha}},\alpha^{\rm an}_{\mid\overline{D}(0,r)^{+}}\big):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

Corollary 7.2.12
.

With the notation above, for every
r\in(0,R),
the following equality holds:

(7.2.21)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r,\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r,\mathbb{C}}})=2\,0pt(\alpha(0))+2\,T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}(r)\\
-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}\big(\alpha^{\rm an}(r\,e^{2\pi it_{1}}),\alpha^{\rm an}(r\,e^{2\pi it_{2}})\big)\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

In the right-hand side of (
7.2.21
), we denote by
\alpha(0)
the point
{\widehat{\alpha}}(0)=\alpha^{\rm an}(0)
of
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{Q}),
by
T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}
the characteristic function of
{\alpha^{\rm an}}
as defined in (
5.4.10
), and by
g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
the Green function for the diagonal of
\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})
defined by (
5.3.2
).

To derive (
7.2.21
) from the expression (
7.2.15
) applied to the morphism
\alpha:=\alpha_{r}
from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}
to
X:=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
and to the Green function
g:=g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})},
observe that the Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{T}}^{\rm cap}_{\pi_{X}}
is then isomorphic to the second tensor power of the Hermitian line bundle
\overline{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(1)
over
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
that defines the usual height (
7.2.20
), and that the equilibrium measure
\mu_{{\overline{D}}(0,1),r}
is the rotation invariant probability measure on
\partial{\overline{D}}(0,r).

Observe that, since
g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}
is non-negative, (
7.2.21
) implies the following upper bound:

(7.2.22)

\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r,\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r,\mathbb{C}}})\leq 2\,0pt(\alpha(0))+2\,T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}(r).

7.3.
Meromorphic maps from f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces to proper arithmetic schemes

The results of the previous sections may be extended to the situation where, instead of a morphism
f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X
from a smooth f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to a quasi-projective scheme or an arithmetic surface

X
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
we consider a meromorphic map:

(7.3.1)

f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to some projective scheme over
{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
.

For lack of suitable available references concerning rational maps or morphisms defined on general Noetherian formal schemes, we will follow a pedestrian approach to define the meromorphic maps
(
7.3.1
), by taking advantage of the two-dimensional nature of the formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
underlying the f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
.

In this section, we denote by:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=\big({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)

a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{\mathcal{O}_{K}}
.

7.3.1.
Regular modifications of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
and meromorphic maps from
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
to projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes

We define a
regular modification
of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as a morphism of formal schemes:

\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

that may be written as a composition:

\nu:=\nu_{1}\circ\dots\circ\nu_{n}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}={\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{n-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{n-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\dots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\nu_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{0}={\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

where
\nu_{i}
is the blowing up of a finite set of closed points of the definition scheme
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}|
of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
.

By construction,
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
is a “regular formal arithmetic surface”, whose reduced scheme of definition
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|
is purely one-dimensional and proper over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

The scheme
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|
admits a unique irreducible component that is finite over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. We shall denote it by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}
; the restriction of
\nu
defines an isomorphism:

\nu_{\mid|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}}:|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|.

The other components of
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
are (isomorphic to) projective lines over some finite fields. We shall denote the union of these vertical components by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{vert}}
.

We shall denote by
{\mathrm{Ind}}(\nu^{-1})
the indeterminacy locus of
\nu^{-1}
, namely the finite set of closed points of
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
in the image of the centers of the morphisms
\nu_{i}
:

{\mathrm{Ind}}(\nu^{-1}):=\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq n}\nu_{1}\circ\dots\circ\nu_{i-1}(C_{i}),

where
C_{i}
denotes the center of the blowing up
\nu_{i}
.

The inverse image by
\nu
of the closed subscheme
{\mathrm{Ind}}(\nu^{-1})
of
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
, hence of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, is an effective divisor on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
, of support
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{vert}}
.

A regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as above is an adic morphism of formal schemes. For every coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}
-module
{\mathcal{F}}
, the direct image
\nu_{\ast}{\mathcal{F}}
is a coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}
-module. When
{\mathcal{F}}
is locally free, its direct image
\nu_{\ast}{\mathcal{F}}
is not always locally free. However it is torsion free, its bidual
(\nu_{\ast}{\mathcal{F}})^{\vee\vee}
is a locally free
{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
-module, the tautological morphism:

\tau:\nu_{\ast}{\mathcal{F}}\longrightarrow(\nu_{\ast}{\mathcal{F}})^{\vee\vee}

is injective, and its cokernel
{\rm coker\,}\tau
is an
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}
-module of finite length, supported by
{\mathrm{Ind}}(\nu^{-1}).

By applying these observations to a line bundle
L^{\prime}
over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},
one obtains:

Proposition 7.3.1
.

For every regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as above and every line bundle
L^{\prime}
over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
, there exists a line bundle
L
over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, a divisor
W
in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
supported by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{vert}}
, and an isomorphism of lines bundles over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
:

\iota:L^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\nu^{\ast}L\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}(W).

When this holds,
L
is canonically isomorphic to
(\nu_{\ast}L^{\prime})^{\vee\vee}
.

We may introduce the
category of regular modifications
of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
by defining a morphism for a regular modification
\nu_{1}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
to a regular modification
\nu_{2}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{2}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as a morphism of formal schemes
\varphi:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{2}
such that
\nu_{2}\circ\varphi=\nu_{1}
.

Actually, if such a morphism exists, it is unique and may be written as a composition of blowing-ups of closed points. Moreover, for any pair of regular modifications:

\nu_{i}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},\quad i=1,2,

there exists a regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
and two morphisms
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
from
\nu
to
\nu_{1}
and
\nu_{2}
respectively. This notably implies that the category of regular modifications is equivalent to a directed set.

Let
X
be a projective scheme over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. We define a
meromorphic map
:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

as a morphism
\widehat{f}_{K}:\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
of (formal) schemes over
K

such that there exists a regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

and a morphism
{\widehat{f}}^{\prime}:\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}\rightarrow X
of (formal) schemes over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:
such that:

{\widehat{f}}^{\prime}_{K}=\widehat{f}_{K}\circ\nu_{K}.

When this holds, we will say that the regular modification
\nu
is
adapted
to
\widehat{f}
.

The set of meromorphic maps from
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
to
X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
may be identified with the limit:

\lim_{\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}\mathrm{Mor}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},X)

of the sets
\mathrm{Mor}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},X)
of morphisms over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, taken over the directed set of formal modifications
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
.

The elements of the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
of formal meromorphic functions on
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, as defined in
[
HM68
]
, may be identified with the meromorphic maps:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}

over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

that are distinct from the “constant” morphism
\infty
. This follows from the desingularization
à la
M. Noether of divisors in the regular formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
; see for instance
[
Abh56
]
and
[
Sha66
, pages 38-44]
.

More generally, consider a morphism
\widehat{f}_{K}:\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
of (formal) schemes over
K
, an affine open neighborhood
U
of
\widehat{f}_{K}(P_{K})
in
X_{K}
, and an embedding of
K
-schemes:

(x_{1},\dots,x_{n}):U{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{N}_{K}.

Then
\widehat{f}_{K}
defines a meromorphic map
\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X
if and only if the elements
x_{1}\circ\widehat{f}_{K},\dots,x_{N}\circ\widehat{f}_{K}
of
{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K})
are elements of the subfield
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}):=\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}):=\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}).

7.3.2.
Meromorphic maps from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes

A
meromorphic map
:

{f}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
from the smooth f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to a projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme
X
is defined by a generalization of the definition of morphisms in
7.1.1
, namely as a
pair:

f:=(\widehat{f},(f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

where:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

is a meromorphic map over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
and where, for every complex embedding
\sigma
of
K
,

f_{\sigma}:V_{\sigma}\longrightarrow X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})

is a complex analytic map. Moreover,
for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
the morphisms from the smooth formal curve
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{\sigma}
to the complex scheme
X_{\sigma}
deduced from
\widehat{f}_{K}
(by the base change
\sigma
) and from
f_{\sigma}
(by considering its “formal germ” at
P_{\sigma}
) are required to coincide.

The discussion in
7.1.1
of the relations between the diverse “Zariski closures of the image” associated to a morphism
f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X
easily extends to the meromorphic maps
f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X
so defined. In particular, it makes sense to say that
f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X
is constant, or that its image is algebraic.

The construction of the pro-Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
associated to a Hermitian vector bundle
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
discussed in
6.1.2
, and the construction of the morphism of (pro-)Hermitian vector bundles :

\varphi_{E}:\pi^{J}_{X\ast}{\overline{E}}\longrightarrow\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}f^{*}\overline{E}

associated to a morphism
f
from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to some projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme
X
equipped with a Hermitian vector bundle
{\overline{E}}
that we discussed in
7.1.2
admit the following generalizations involving regular modifications.

Consider a regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
as above. The pair:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}:=({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},(V_{\sigma},O_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

defines a generalized smooth formal-analytic surface. There is an obvious notion of a vector bundle:

\widetilde{E}:=(\widehat{E},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}),

and of a Hermitian vector bundle:

\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=(\widehat{E},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
, defined as in
6.1.2
, where
\widehat{E}
is now a vector bundle over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
.

Let
\mu
be a positive volume form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, invariant under complex conjugation, as in
6.1.2
. Then to a Hermitian vector bundle
\widetilde{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
, we may attach:

(7.3.2)

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}:=\Big(\Gamma({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\widehat{E}),(\Gamma_{L^{2}}(V_{\sigma},\mu_{\sigma};E_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma}),\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\Big),

where
\widehat{\eta}_{\sigma}
is defined as in
6.1.2
, with obvious minor modifications.
An extension of the proof in
[
Bos20
, 10.6.2]
shows that it is a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, as in the case where
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
is
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
considered in
6.1.2
above.

The construction (
7.3.2
) of the direct image on
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
of some Hermitian vector bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
is related as follows to the construction of the direct image of Hermitian torsion free coherent sheaves and vector bundles over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
.

Consider a Hermitian vector bundle:

{\overline{E}}^{\prime}:=\big(\widehat{E}^{\prime},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)

over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
. The direct image
\nu_{\ast}{\widehat{E}}^{\prime}
of the underlying vector bundle
{\widehat{E}}^{\prime}
over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
by the modification
\nu
is a torsion free coherent sheaf over
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
. Moreover its “restriction”
(\nu_{\ast}{\widehat{E}}^{\prime})_{K}
to
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}_{K}\simeq{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}
may be identified with
{\widehat{E}}^{\prime}_{K}
. We therefore define a Hermitian torsion free coherent sheaf over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
by letting:

\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}:=\big(\nu_{\ast}\widehat{E}^{\prime},(E_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big).

The identification of coherent sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}
-modules:

\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\ast}\nu_{\ast}{\widehat{E}}^{\prime}\simeq\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\ast}{\widehat{E}}^{\prime}

extends to an identification:

(7.3.3)

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}(\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime})\simeq\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}.

As explained in
6.1.2
, the left-hand side of (
7.3.3
) is a pro-Hermitian vector bundle over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and therefore
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
also is. Moreover, as shown by Proposition
6.1.1
, the properties of the pro-Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
— for instance its
\theta
-finiteness — are closely related to the properties of
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}
, where:

{\overline{E}}:=(\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime})^{\vee\vee}

is the Hermitian vector bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
deduced from
\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
by biduality.

Once the direct image (
7.3.2
) has been introduced, Proposition
7.1.1
immediately extends to the situation where
f
is a meromorphic map:

f:=(\widehat{f},f^{\rm an}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

from
X
to a reduced flat projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme
X
equipped with some Hermitian vector bundle
{\overline{E}}
.

Indeed, if
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
denotes a regular modification of
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
adapted to the meromorphic map:

\widehat{f}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

and if:

f^{\prime}:=(\widehat{f}^{\prime},f^{\rm an}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X

denotes the associated
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphism, then we may consider the Hermitian vector bundle
f^{\prime\ast}{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
and its direct image
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}f^{\prime\ast}{\overline{E}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. It is readily seen to be “independent” of the choice of the adapted modification
\nu
. Moreover the pull-back of sections of
E
by
f^{\prime}
defines a morphism:

\varphi_{E}:\pi^{J}_{X\ast}\widetilde{\overline{E}}\longrightarrow\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}f^{\prime*}\overline{E}

of (pro-)Hermitian vector bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and the last assertion of Proposition
7.1.1
still holds.

7.3.3.
Meromorphic maps from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to projective arithmetic surfaces and arithmetic intersections numbers

subsection

Assume that
X
is an integral normal projective arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and consider a non-constant meromorphic map:
