\alpha:({\widehat{\alpha}},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X.

As before, we shall denote by
\alpha^{\rm an}
the complex analytic map from
V_{\mathbb{C}}
to
X(\mathbb{C})
defined by the maps
\alpha_{\sigma}
, and by
e(\alpha)
the ramification index of the morphism
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
.

In this subsection, we discuss the extension to this framework of the constructions and results of Section
7.2
.

Let us choose a regular modification
\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\rightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
adapted to
{\widehat{\alpha}}
, and let us denote by:

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X

the morphism of (formal) schemes over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
that defines
{\widehat{\alpha}}
.

Let
(D,g)
be an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
. The inverse image
\nu^{\ast}D
is a divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
supported by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|,
and its direct image by the proper map
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}:|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|\rightarrow X
defines a divisor:

\Gamma_{{\widehat{\alpha}}\ast}D:={\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}\nu^{\ast}D

in
Z^{1}(X)
. It is readily seen to be independent of the choice of the adapted regular modification
\nu
, and we may define:

\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(D,g):=(\Gamma_{{\widehat{\alpha}}\ast}D,\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)=(\Gamma_{{\widehat{\alpha}}\ast}D,(\alpha_{\sigma\ast}g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}).

It is an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}(X)
, and this construction defines a morphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-modules:

\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}:{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\longrightarrow{\overline{Z}}^{1}(X),

which is compatible with the positivity of Arakelov divisors.

If
f:X\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
is a dominant, or equivalently surjective, morphism of integral normal projective arithmetic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, then:

f\circ\alpha:=(f\circ{\widehat{\alpha}},(f_{\sigma}\circ\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X^{\prime}

is a non-constant meromorphic map, and for every Arakelov divisor
(D,g)
in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, the following equality holds in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}(X^{\prime})
:

(7.3.4)

(f\circ\alpha)_{\ast}(D,g)=f_{\ast}\alpha_{\ast}(D,g).

The proof is straighforward.

We shall denote by
P^{\prime}
the section of the structure morphism:

\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

defined as the proper transform of
P
. It is the inverse of the isomorphism:

\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\mid|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}}:|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}.

The divisor
\nu^{\ast}P
in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
may be written:

(7.3.5)

\nu^{\ast}P=P^{\prime}+V^{\prime},

where
V^{\prime}
is an effective divisor supported by
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{vert}}
.

As before, we denote by
Q
the section
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}\circ P
of the structure morphism
\pi_{X}:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. The inverse image
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}(Q)
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, and may be written:

(7.3.6)

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}(Q)=e(\alpha)P^{\prime}+R^{\prime},

where
R^{\prime}
is an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
such that
|R^{\prime}|\cap P^{\prime}=|R^{\prime}|\cap|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}
is a finite set of closed points, and therefore
|R^{\prime}|\cap|\nu^{\ast}P|
is proper over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

The direct image

\pi_{|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|\ast}(R^{\prime}\cdot\nu^{\ast}P)
by the structure morphism
\pi_{|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|}:|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|\rightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

of the
0
-dimensional intersection cycle of
R^{\prime}
and
\nu^{\ast}P
, which is defined up to vertical linear equivalence, is a well defined
0
-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-cycle in
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. It is readily seen to be independent of the choice of the adapted regular modification
\nu
, and consequently its Arakelov degree:

(7.3.7)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,R^{\prime}\cdot\nu^{\ast}P

also is.

Observe that the Arakelov degree (
7.3.7
) is non-negative. Actually, for any effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
D
in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
that does not contain
P^{\prime}
, the Arakelov degree:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,D\cdot\nu^{\ast}P

is well-defined and non-negative. This non-negativity indeed follows from the fact that, for every component
W
of
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{hor}}
, the intersection number
\widehat{\rm deg}\,W\cdot\nu^{\ast}P
vanishes.

The direct image
{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}^{\prime}V^{\prime}
is a vertical effective divisor in
X
, and its inverse image
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}^{\prime}V^{\prime}
is an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor in
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
. Here again
|{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}^{\prime}V|\cap P^{\prime}
is a finite set of closed points, the direct image
\pi_{|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|\ast}({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}^{\prime}V^{\prime}\cdot\nu^{\ast}P^{\prime})
is a well-defined 0-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}
-cycle in
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
which does not depend of the choice of
\nu,
and its Arakelov degree:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\ast}^{\prime}V^{\prime}\cdot\nu^{\ast}P^{\prime}

also is non-negative.

Definition 7.3.2
.

With the above notation, we define:

(7.3.8)

{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X):=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(R^{\prime}+{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot\nu^{\ast}P.

One easily checks that the invariant
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)
also admits the following expressions:

(7.3.9)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rm deg}\,\big({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}(\nu^{\ast}(P)\big)-e(\alpha)\,P^{\prime})\cdot\nu^{\ast}P

(7.3.10)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\rm deg}\,\big({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}(\nu^{\ast}P)-e(\alpha)\,\nu^{\ast}P\big)\cdot\nu^{\ast}P,

which extend the equality (
7.2.7
) to the situation, and are formally similar to (
5.2.3
).

Observe that (
7.3.9
) makes clear that
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)
is non-negative, and (
7.3.10
) that it is independent of the choice of the regular modification
\nu
adapted to
{\widehat{\alpha}}
.

As in
7.2.3
, let us consider a family
g:=(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
of Green functions for the points
P_{\sigma}
in the Riemann surfaces
V^{+}_{\sigma}
such that
(P,g)
is
an Arakelov divisor in
{\overline{Z}}^{1}_{c}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, and the associated Hermitian line bundle:

{\overline{N}}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}:=(N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})

defined by the associated capacitary metrics
\|.\|_{g_{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{cap}}
.

The following proposition is the generalization of Proposition
7.2.6
to meromorphic maps.

Proposition 7.3.3
.

With the notation above, the following equality holds:

(7.3.11)

\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g)\cdot\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g)=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}+\mathrm{Ex}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big)+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma}).

When the Green functions
g_{\sigma}
are the equilibrium potentials
g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}
, the equality (
7.3.11
) becomes the following generalization of (
7.2.13
):

(7.3.12)

\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}+\mathrm{Ex}\big(\widehat{\alpha}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big)\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Ex}\big(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})\big).

Proof of Proposition
7.3.3
.

We may choose a positive integer
N
such that the divisor
NQ
and
N{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}
are Cartier. Then we may define a Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{L}}
over
X
by:

(7.3.13)

{\overline{L}}:=\overline{\mathcal{O}}(N\Gamma_{\alpha*}P,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)=\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ+N{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime},N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g).

We want to show that
N^{-2}{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}
equals the right-hand side of (
7.3.11
).

In the present context, the relations (
7.2.10
), (
7.2.11
), and (
7.2.12
) in the proof of Lemma
7.2.7
are replaced by:

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}(NQ)=e(\alpha)NP^{\prime}+NR^{\prime},

I:{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(NQ)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}(e(\alpha)NP^{\prime})\otimes{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}(NR^{\prime}),

and:

I_{\mid P^{\prime}}:Q^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{X}(NQ)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}}\big(e(\alpha)NP^{\prime}\big)\otimes P^{\prime\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}(NR^{\prime}),

and the conclusion (
7.2.9
) of Lemma
7.2.7
becomes:

N^{-1}\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}\overline{\mathcal{O}}(NQ,N\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{\prime*}\overline{N}_{P^{\prime},g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot R^{\prime}+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{\sigma}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma*}(Q_{\sigma})-eP_{\sigma}}.

Therefore:

(7.3.14)

N^{-1}\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}{\overline{L}}=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{\prime*}\overline{N}_{P^{\prime},g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot R^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{\sigma}\,\delta_{\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{\sigma*}(Q_{\sigma})-eP_{\sigma}}.

Moreover, we have:

\displaystyle N^{-2}\,{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\,{\overline{L}}\cdot(Q+{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime},\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g)

(7.3.15)

\displaystyle=N^{-1}\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q^{\ast}{\overline{L}}+N^{-1}\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,L\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}+\int_{X(\mathbb{C})}\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g\,\omega(\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g).

From (
7.3.14
) and (
7.3.3
), we get:

(7.3.16)

N^{-2}\,{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}=e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{\prime*}\overline{N}_{P^{\prime},g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot R^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,(Q+{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}\\
+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Ex}(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma}).

Using the isomorphism:

P^{\ast}N_{P}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime\ast}\nu^{\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(P)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime\ast}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}(P^{\prime}+V^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime\ast}N_{P}^{\prime}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{O}}(P^{\prime}\cdot V^{\prime}),

we get the following equality between arithmetic degrees:

(7.3.17)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P^{\prime},g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\ast}{\overline{N}}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}-\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot V^{\prime}.

Using (
7.3.16
) and (
7.3.17
), we see that the validity of (
7.3.11
)
follows from the following equality between intersection numbers:

(7.3.18)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,(R^{\prime}+{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime\ast}{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot\nu^{\ast}P=-e(\alpha)\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot V^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot R^{\prime}+\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}\\
+\widehat{\rm deg}\,(Q+{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}.

This is a straighforward consequence of (
7.3.5
) and (
7.3.6
) and of the projection formula, which implies the following relations:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,Q\cdot\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}=\widehat{\rm deg}\,\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}P^{\prime}\cdot\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{\prime}\cdot\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime\ast}\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime},

and

\widehat{\rm deg}\,(Q+\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime}=\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime\ast}Q+\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime\ast}\widehat{\alpha}^{\prime}_{\ast}V^{\prime})\cdot V^{\prime}.\qed

Let
U
be an integral normal arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, possibly non-projective. There exists an open imbedding of
U
into some integral normal projective arithmetic surface
X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. One might define a meromorphic map:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},\alpha^{\rm an}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow U

as a meromorphic map:

\alpha^{\prime}:=({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime{\rm an}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

such that the morphism
\alpha^{\prime}_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
does not factor through
(X\setminus U)_{K}
. This definition may be seen to be independent of the choice of the compactification
X
of
U
. However the self-intersection
\Gamma_{\alpha^{\prime}\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\Gamma_{\alpha^{\prime}\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
may in general depend of this choice.

This dependence forbids us to define the self-intersection
\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
when the range
U
of
\alpha
is not projective,
e.g.
affine, and the compactification
X
is not specified.

Chapter 8
Pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces I: degree bounds, algebraicity, and the field
{\mathcal{M}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}})

This chapter is devoted to the properties of pseudoconcave arithmetic surfaces and of the morphisms from those to arithmetic schemes. These properties constitute the main results of this memoir, and are established by transposing in the arithmetic setting the arguments already introduced in a geometric framework in Part 1, by using the tools developed in Part 2.

Notably we derive a bound on the degree of a morphism between arithmetic surfaces which is an arithmetic analogue of the geometric bounds in Propositions
1.3.1
and

2.1.6
, and where the role of the auxiliary complex analytic surface
{\mathcal{V}}
is played by a f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
.

We improve on the algebraicity results concerning smooth morphisms from pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ surfaces to arithmetic schemes established in
[
Bos20
, Chapter 10]
by proving in Theorem
8.3.1
and Corollary
8.3.2

some finiteness results concerning the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
of meromorphic functions on a pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, which play the role of Theorems
2.2.5
and
2.3.1
. We conclude this chapter by discussing how these finiteness results contain as a special case
the arithmetic holonomicity theorem of
[
CDT21
]
.

In the next chapter, we shall complement these results by some arithmetic analogues of the finiteness results concerning
the universal meromorphic map
\varphi:{\mathcal{V}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
and
the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
introduced in Subsection
2.3.3
, and of the Lefschetz-Nori theorems on fundamental groups of algebraic surfaces established in Proposition
1.3.2
and Theorem
2.2.4
.

In this chapter, we denote by
K
a number field, and by
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
its ring of integers.

8.1.
The invariant
D(\alpha)
. Degree bounds on morphisms between arithmetic surfaces

8.1.1.
The invariant
D(\alpha)

Definitions

Recall from Section
6.3
that a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
is called pseudoconcave when the metrized normal bundle
{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
satisfies the following positivity condition:

\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}>0.

Definition 8.1.1
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

For every non-constant morphism over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to some integral normal surface
X
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
we let:

(8.1.1)

D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big):=\frac{\alpha_{\ast}\big(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big)\cdot\alpha_{\ast}\big(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big)}{\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}

Similarly, for every non-constant meromorphic map over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to some integral normal projective surface
X
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
we let:

(8.1.2)

D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big):=\frac{\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}\big(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big)\cdot\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}\big(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big)}{\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}

According to Corollary
7.2.8
and (
7.2.13
), and to Proposition

7.3.3
and
(
7.3.12
), the invariants
D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)
and
D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big)
admit the following expressions:

(8.1.3)

D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)=e(\alpha)+\big(\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\big)^{-1}\Big({\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})\Big)

and:

(8.1.4)

D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big)=e(\alpha)+\big(\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\big)^{-1}\Big({\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})\Big),

where, as usual,
e(\alpha)
denotes the ramification index of
\alpha_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
.

Together with the non-negativity of the overflow invariants
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
,
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)
, and
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})\rightarrow X_{\sigma})
, the relations (
8.1.3
) and (
8.1.4
) imply the following lower bounds:

(8.1.5)

D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X\big)\geq e(\alpha)\quad\mbox{and}\quad D\big(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\big)\geq e(\alpha).

Examples

Thanks to the expression of the Archimedean overflow in Theorem
5.4.1
and to its applications in Subsections
5.4.2
and
7.2.4
,
one gets explicit formulae for the invariant
D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is the f.-a.​​ surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,r),\psi)
over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
associated to a formal series in
G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R}):=\mathbb{R}^{\ast}T+T^{2}\mathbb{R}[[T]]
, and when
X
is
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
or
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Consider for instance the situation in paragraph
7.2.4
. Namely suppose that we are given a series
\psi
in
G_{\mathrm{for}}(\mathbb{R})
, a morphism of formal schemes:

{\widehat{\alpha}}:{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}},

and
a meromorphic function, defined on the open disk
D(0,R)
of radius
R\in(0,+\infty]
:

\alpha^{{\rm an}}:D(0,R)\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C}).

We also assume that
{\widehat{\alpha}}
and
\alpha^{\rm an}
are not constant and satisfy the relation:

{\widehat{\alpha}}=\alpha^{\rm an}\circ\psi.

For every
r\in(0,R),
we introduce the smooth
f.-a.​​ surface over
{\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}:={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,r),\psi),

and the morphism:

\alpha_{r}:=\big({\widehat{\alpha}},\alpha^{\rm an}_{\mid\overline{D}(0,r)^{+}}\big):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}.

Then we have:

(8.1.6)

\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}=\log(r/|\psi^{\prime}(0)|),

and consequently
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}
is pseudoconcave if and only if:

r>|\psi^{\prime}(0)|.

The invariant
D\big(\alpha_{r}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)
is defined for every
r\in(|\psi^{\prime}(0)|,R)
. According to (
7.2.21
)
and (
8.1.6
), it satisfies:

(8.1.7)

D\big(\alpha_{r}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)=2\,\big(\log(r/|\psi^{\prime}(0)|)\big)^{-1}\big(0pt(\alpha(0))+T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}(r)\big)\\
-\big(\log(r/|\psi^{\prime}(0)|)\big)^{-1}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}g_{\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C})}\big(\alpha^{\rm an}(r\,e^{2\pi it_{1}}),\alpha^{\rm an}(r\,e^{2\pi it_{2}})\big)\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

See (
7.2.20
) and (
5.4.10
) for the definitions of the height
0pt(\alpha(0))
and of the Nevanlinna characteristic function
T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}.
Observe also that (
8.1.7
) implies the following upper-bound:

(8.1.8)

D\big(\alpha_{r}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)\leq 2\,\big(\log(r/|\psi^{\prime}(0)|)\big)^{-1}\big(0pt(\alpha(0))+T_{\alpha^{\rm an}}(r)\big).

When
{\widehat{\alpha}}
is a morphism from
{\rm Spf\,}\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
to
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}
(equivalently when
{\widehat{\alpha}}
is defined by a formal series in
\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
) and
\alpha^{{\rm an}}
takes its values in
\mathbb{C}
, then using (
7.2.19
) we also get the following equality:

(8.1.9)

D\big(\alpha_{r}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)=2\,\big(\log(r/|\psi^{\prime}(0)|)\big)^{-1}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\log\left|\alpha^{\rm an}(re^{2\pi it_{1}})-\alpha^{\rm an}(re^{2\pi it_{2}})\right|\,dt_{1}\,dt_{2}.

8.1.2.
Pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ surfaces and degree bounds on maps between arithmetic surfaces

We may now establish the arithmetic counterparts of Propositions
1.3.1
and
2.1.6
and of the degree bound (
2.1.11
) and (
2.1.16
) that constitute the central result of this memoir.

Theorem 8.1.2
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and let
U
and
V
be two integral normal arithmetic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. Consider a commutative diagram of morphisms over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\textstyle{V\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{U.}

If
\alpha
is non-constant, then
\beta
also is non-constant,
f
is dominant and generically finite, and its degree
\deg f
satisfies the upper bound:

(8.1.10)

\deg f\leq\frac{D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)}{D(\beta:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow V)}.

Recall that by definition
\deg f
is the degree of the finite extension of fields:

f^{\ast}:\kappa(X)=K(X_{K})\,{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\,\kappa(Y)=K(Y_{K}).

Observe also that (
8.1.10
) immediately implies the following upper bound on
\deg f
:

(8.1.11)

\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)/e(\beta)\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U).

Proof.

When
\alpha
is non-constant, then
\beta
also is clearly non-constant, and
f_{K}
is non-constant. This implies that
f
is dominant and generically finite.

If we let:

{\overline{A}}:=\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\overline{B}}:=\beta_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}),

then we have:

{\overline{A}}=f_{\ast}{\overline{B}}

by Proposition
7.2.1
. Moreover, according to the definition and to the positivity of
D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)
, we have:

\displaystyle{\overline{A}}\cdot{\overline{A}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\alpha_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})

\displaystyle=D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)\;\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}>0.

Similarly we have:

\displaystyle{\overline{B}}\cdot{\overline{B}}

\displaystyle=\beta_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\cdot\beta_{\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})

\displaystyle=D(\beta:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow V)\;\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}>0.

Therefore the upper bound (
8.1.10
) follows from the upper bound (
3.5.19
):

\deg f\leq\frac{{\overline{A}}\cdot{\overline{A}}}{{\overline{B}}\cdot{\overline{B}}}

established in Theorem
3.5.10
.
∎

Theorem
8.1.2
admits the following variant concerning meromorphic maps from pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ surfaces to projective arithmetic surfaces:

Theorem 8.1.3
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and let
X
and
Y
be two integral normal projective arithmetic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. Consider a commutative diagram of morphisms over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

(8.1.12)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 32.38194pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.39583pt\raise-15.63889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.39583pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.09853pt\raise-26.77083pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.2437pt\raise-9.52779pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\beta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.39716pt\raise-5.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X,}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

where
X
and
Y
are integral projective arithmetic surfaces over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, with
X
normal, where
\alpha
and
\beta
are meromorphic maps over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and where
f
is a rational map over of
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

If
\alpha
is non-constant, then
\beta
also is non-constant,
f
is dominant and generically finite, and its degree
\deg f
satisfies the upper bound:

(8.1.13)

\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)/e(\beta).

Recall that the data of a rational map
f:Y\dashrightarrow X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
is equivalent to the data of a
K
-morphism of curves:
f_{K}:Y_{K}\rightarrow X_{K}
and that the degree of
f
coincides with the degree
[K(Y_{K}):f_{K}^{\ast}K(X_{K})]
of this morphism.
The commutativity of the diagram (
8.1.12
) may be defined by the commutativity of the following diagram of (formal) schemes over
K
:

\textstyle{Y_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{K}}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\widehat{\alpha}}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}
\textstyle{X_{K}.}

Proof.

After possibly replacing
Y
by the normalization of the closure of the graph of
f
, we may assume that
f
is a not only a rational map, but an actual morphism from
Y
to
X
. Indeed this reduction does not modify
f_{K}
and its degree, nor the meromorphic character of
\widehat{\beta}
, nor the ramification index
e(\beta)
of
\beta_{K}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K}\rightarrow Y_{K}
.
1
1

1

However it may modify
D(\beta:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow V)
.

Then we may define:

{\overline{A}}:=\Gamma_{\alpha\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\overline{B}}:=\Gamma_{\beta\ast}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}).

We still have:

{\overline{A}}=f_{\ast}{\overline{B}}

by (
7.3.4
). Moreover:

{\overline{A}}\cdot{\overline{A}}=D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X)\;\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}

and

{\overline{B}}\cdot{\overline{B}}=D(\beta:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow Y)\;\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\geq e(\beta)\,\widehat{\rm deg}\,{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}.

Therefore the upper bound (
8.1.13
) again follows from (
3.5.19
).
∎

8.2.
Algebraicity of maps from pseudoconcave f.-a.​ arithmetic surfaces to arithmetic schemes

8.2.1.
Upper bounds on
h^{0}_{\theta}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D})
and algebraicity

The following algebraicity theorem is an arithmetic analogue of the algebraicity results established in a geometric framework in Proposition
1.4.3
and Proposition
2.1.3
.

Theorem 8.2.1
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface. For every quasi-projective (resp. projective)
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme and any morphism

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow X

(resp. any meromorphic map:

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X
)
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
the image of
\alpha
is algebraic.

When
\alpha
is a morphism to a quasi-projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme, that is the main result in
[
Bos20
, Chapter 10]
; see
loc. cit.

Theorem 10.8.1. In this section, we explain how the proof in
[
Bos20
]
may be extended to cover the case of a meromorphic map
\alpha
from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to a projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme. We also derive an alternative proof of the degree bound in (
8.1.11
):

\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)

from the results of
[
Bos20
]
, analogue to the alternative proof of the geometric degree bound (
1.3.7
) given in Subsection
1.4.1
.

As in the geometric situations studied in Subsections
1.4.2
and
2.1.1
, the algebraicity theorem
8.2.1
is a consequence of a finiteness result concerning the spaces of sections attached some Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{M}}
over a pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
and to its tensor powers
M^{\otimes D}
, and of the asymptotic behavior of the “dimensions”
{h^{0}_{\theta}}\big({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D}\big)
of these spaces.

Our main tool will be the following theorem, which is basically established in
[
Bos20
, Sections 10.5 and 10.7]
, and constitutes an arithmetic counterpart of
Propositions
1.4.1
and
2.1.1
.

Theorem 8.2.2
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=\big({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)
be a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface, and let
\mu
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
positive volume form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
invariant under complex conjugation.

(1) For every Hermitian vector bundle
{\overline{E}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, the pro-Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}
is
\theta
-finite, and we may therefore define:

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{E}}):={h^{0}_{\theta}}\big(\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}\big).

(2) For every Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{M}}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
, when
D\in\mathbb{N}
goes to infinity, we have:

(8.2.1)

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D})=O(D^{2}).

More precisely, when
\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}{\overline{M}}<0
, we have:

(8.2.2)

\lim_{D\rightarrow+\infty}h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D})=0,

and in general:

(8.2.3)

\limsup_{D\rightarrow+\infty}D^{-2}\,h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D})\leq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\big({\overline{M}}\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\big)^{2}}{\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}.

In the right-hand side of (
8.2.3
),
P
denotes the canonical section of the structure morphism
\pi_{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
,
(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is the Arakelov divisor on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
defined in Subsection
6.2.2
by the equilibrium potentials
(g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
introduced in
4.3.1
, and
{\overline{N}}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
the metrized normal bundle of
P
in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
defined by (
6.2.3
). The arithmetic intersection number
{\overline{M}}\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})
has been defined in Subsection
6.2.3
; explicitly, we have:

\overline{M}\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}):=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{*}\overline{M}+\int_{V_{\mathbb{C}}}g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}\,c_{1}(\overline{M})=\widehat{\rm deg}\,P^{*}\overline{M}+\sum_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\int_{V_{\sigma}}g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}\,c_{1}({\overline{M}}_{\sigma}).

Theorem
8.2.2
follows from Theorem 10.7.1 in
[
Bos20
]
and from its proof. Indeed, the limit estimates (
8.2.2
) and (
8.2.3
) follow from the proof of Theorem 10.7.1 in
[
Bos20
, 10.7.4 pp. 286 – 287]
, and from the bound on the constant
C_{\eta}
in Lemma 10.7.2 provided by the Schwarz lemma on a compact Riemann surface with boundary in
[
Bos20
, 10.5.5]
.

To handle the case of a meromorphic map
\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X
in Theorem
8.2.1
only, we will use the following partial generalization
2
2

2

The proof of Theorem
8.2.3
will rely on
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.7.1]
, and not on the more precise version, including the estimate (
8.2.3
), stated in Theorem
8.2.2
.
of Theorem
8.2.2
.

Theorem 8.2.3
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}:=\big({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)
be a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface, let
\mu
be a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
positive volume form on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
invariant under complex conjugation, and let

\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

be a regular modification.

(1) For every Hermitian vector bundle
{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}:=\big({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma},\iota_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)
, the pro-Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
is
\theta
-finite, and we may therefore define:

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu;{\overline{E}}^{\prime}):={h^{0}_{\theta}}\big(\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}\big).

(2) For every Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{M}}^{\prime}
on
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
, when
D\in\mathbb{N}
goes to infinity, we have:

(8.2.4)

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\prime\otimes D})=O(D^{2}).

Proof.

Let
{\overline{E}}^{\prime}:=(\widehat{E}^{\prime},(E^{\prime}_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
be a Hermitian vector bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
. We may define the Hermitian torsion free coherent sheaf over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
:

\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}:=\big(\nu_{\ast}\widehat{E}^{\prime},(E^{\prime}_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big),

and its bidual:

{\overline{E}}:=(\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime})^{\vee\vee}:=\big((\nu_{\ast}\widehat{E})^{\vee\vee},(E^{\prime}_{\sigma},\varphi_{\sigma},\|.\|_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big).

As already observed in
7.3.2
, the direct image
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}^{\prime}}
may be identified with

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
. According to Proposition
6.1.1
, the
\theta
-finiteness of
\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime}
follows from the one of

\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu)\ast}{\overline{E}}
, itself established in
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.7.1]
, and stated in Theorem
8.2.2
, (1), above. Moreover the following inequality holds:

(8.2.5)

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu;{\overline{E}}^{\prime})=h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\nu_{\ast}{\overline{E}}^{\prime})\leq h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{E}}).

Let
{\overline{M}}^{\prime}
be a Hermitian line bundle over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
. According to Proposition
7.3.1
, it may be written
\nu^{\ast}{\overline{M}}(W)
for some Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{M}}
over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
and some divisor
W
in
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}
supported by the union
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}|_{\mathrm{vert}}
of the vertical components of
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
. For every integer
D
,
{\overline{M}}^{\prime\otimes D}
is isomorphic to
\nu^{\ast}{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D}(DW)
, and the bidual of
\nu_{\ast}{\overline{M}}^{\prime\otimes D}
is isomorphic to
{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D}
.

Applied to
{\overline{E}}^{\prime}={\overline{M}}^{\prime\otimes D},
the inequality (
8.2.5
) reads:

(8.2.6)

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\prime\otimes D})\leq h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D}).

As proved in
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.7.1]
, and stated in Theorem
8.2.2
, (2), when
D\in\mathbb{N}
goes to infinity, we have:

h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;{\overline{M}}^{\otimes D})=O(D^{2}).

Together with (
8.2.6
), this establishes (
8.2.4
).
∎

Let us complete the proof of Theorem
8.2.1
by establishing the algebraicity of the image of a meromorphic map:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, when
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
and
X
are respectively a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ surface and a projective scheme over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. The following argument is a minor variant of the proof of
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.8.1]
, but we include it for the sake of completeness.

With the above notation, we want to prove that the Zariski closure
\overline{{\rm im\,}{\widehat{\alpha}}}
of the image of
{\widehat{\alpha}}
satisfies:

\dim\overline{{\rm im\,}{\widehat{\alpha}}}\leq 2.

To achieve this, we may assume that
X
coincides with
\overline{\mathrm{im}\widehat{\alpha}},
and therefore is an integral projective flat scheme over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

Moreover we may chose a Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{L}}
over
X
such that, if we let, for every integer
D
:
3
3

3

See paragraph
7.1.2
for the definition of the Hermitian vector bundle
\pi^{J}_{X*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}
.

h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,\overline{L}^{\otimes D}):=h^{0}_{\theta}(\pi^{J}_{X*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}),

then the following condition is satisfied:

(8.2.7)

\liminf_{D\rightarrow+\infty}D^{-\dim X}h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,\overline{L}^{\otimes D})>0.

See
[
Bos20
, 10.3]
for an elementary construction of a Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{L}}
satisfying (
8.2.7
). Actually, as shown in
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.3.2]
, any Hermitian line bundle
\overline{L}
over
X
that is arithmetically ample in the sense of Zhang will do.

We may choose a regular modification:

\nu:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}

adapted to the meromorphic map
{\widehat{\alpha}}
, and denote by:

{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X

the morphism of (formal) schemes defining
{\widehat{\alpha}}
.

Finally we may choose a positive smooth volume form
\mu
on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, invariant under complex conjugation, such that, for every complex embedding
\sigma
of
K
, we have
\mu(V_{\sigma})\leq 1.

As observed in
7.1.2
, Proposition
7.1.1
, and in
7.3.2
, pulling back sections of
\overline{L}^{\otimes D}
on
X
along the morphism
\alpha^{\prime}:=\big({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:H\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)
defines a morphism:

\eta_{D}:\pi^{J}_{X*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}\longrightarrow\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\prime},\nu)*}\alpha^{\prime*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}

of (pro-)Hermitian vector bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
with Archimedean norms bounded above by
1
. Moreover, since
X
is the Zariski closure of the image of
{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}
, the morphisms
\eta_{D}
are injective, and consequently the following estimates hold:

(8.2.8)

h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,\overline{L}^{\otimes D})\leq h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\alpha^{\prime*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}).

The asymptotic bound (
8.2.4
) applied to
\overline{M}=\alpha^{\prime*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}
shows that the right-hand side of (
8.2.8
) is
O(D^{2})
when
D
goes to
+\infty
. Together with (
8.2.7
), this implies:

\dim\overline{\mathrm{im}\,{\widehat{\alpha}}^{\prime}}=\dim X\leq 2,

and finishes the proof.

8.2.2.
An alternative proof of the estimate
\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)

As already mentioned, it is possible to establish the inequality

\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)

in Theorem
8.1.2
by a proof similar to the one in Subsection
1.4.1
.
The reader may compare the argument below with the argument in
[
Cha22
, Sections 4 and 5]
.

Nef and big Hermitian line bundles on projective arithmetic surfaces

Recall that a Hermitian line bundle
\overline{L}=(L,\|.\|)
, defined by a
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathrm{b}\Delta}}
metric
\|.\|
, over an integral normal projective arithmetic surface
X
is
nef
when, for every effective
4
4

4

An Arakelov divisor
(Z,g)
is effective when the divisor
Z
is effective and the Green function is non-negative.
Arakelov divisor
(Z,g)
on
X
, we have:

\overline{L}.(Z,g)\geq 0.

A Hermitian line bundle
\overline{L}
is nef if and only if the two following conditions hold:

(1)

for every closed integral one-dimensional subscheme
C
of
X
:

h_{\overline{L}}(C):=\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{L}_{|C}\geq 0;

(2)

the measure
c_{1}(\overline{L})
on
X(\mathbb{C})
is semi-positive.

A nef Hermitian line bundle is
big
if, additionally:

\overline{L}\cdot\overline{L}>0.

Using that any divisor on
X
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier, we may define similarly nef,
5
5

5

An effective Arakelov divisor
(Z,g)
is nef if and only if
\omega(g)\geq 0
and
\widehat{\rm deg}\,(\overline{\mathcal{O}}(Z,g)\mid C)\geq 0
for every component
C
of
Z
; see
[
Bos99
, Proposition 6.9]
.
and nef and big, Arakelov
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors on
X
. The following proposition is a straightforward consequence of the basic properties of the Arakelov intersection pairing.

Proposition 8.2.4
.

. Let
f:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
a dominant (hence surjective) morphism between two integral normal projective arithmetic surfaces. If a Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{L}}
on
X
is nef (resp. nef and big), then its pull-back
f^{\ast}{\overline{L}}
is a nef (resp. nef and big) Hermitian line bundle on
X^{\prime}
.

The following “arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula” is a direct consequence of
[
Zha95
, Theorem 1.4]
by the arguments in the proof of
[
Bos20
, Theorem 10.3.2]
.

Theorem 8.2.5
.

Let
\overline{L}
be a big and nef Hermitian line bundle over an integral, normal projective arithmetic surface
X
over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
. When the integer
D
goes to
+\infty,
we have:

(8.2.9)

h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,\overline{L}^{\otimes D})={\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}\;D^{2}/2+o(D^{2}).

Part (1) in the following proposition is an arithmetic analogue of Corollary
1.4.2
.
Its proof will be a variation on the algebraicity proof in
8.2.1
above, where instead of the crude estimates
(
8.2.1
) and (
8.2.7
), we shall use (
8.2.3
) and (
8.2.9
).

Proposition 8.2.6
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave smooth formal-analytic arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
and let

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X

be a nonconstant
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to an integral normal projective arithmetic surface
X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

(1) For every Hermitian line bundle
{\overline{L}}
over
X
that is nef and big, the following inequality holds:

(8.2.10)

{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}\leq\frac{\big(\alpha^{*}{\overline{L}}\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})\big)^{2}}{\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}.

(2) The Arakelov divisor on
X
:

\alpha_{*}\big(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big):=\big(\widehat{\alpha}_{*}(P),\alpha^{\mathrm{an}}_{*}g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}}\big)

is nef and big.

Proof of Proposition
8.2.6
.

(1) We choose a
\mathcal{C}^{\infty}
positive volume form
\mu
on
V_{\mathbb{C}}
, invariant under complex conjugation, such that, for every complex embedding
\sigma
of
K
,
\mu(V_{\sigma})\leq 1.

As in paragraph
8.2.1
, for every nonnegative integer
D
, pulling back sections of
L^{\otimes D}
on
X
by
\alpha
defines a morphism of (pro-)Hermitian vector bundles over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
with Archimedean norms bounded above by
1
:

\eta_{D}:\pi^{J}_{X*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}\longrightarrow\pi^{L^{2}}_{({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\nu)*}\alpha^{*}\overline{L}^{\otimes D}.

Since
\alpha
is not constant, the Zariski closure
\overline{\mathrm{im}{\widehat{\alpha}}}
of the image of
{\widehat{\alpha}}
in
X
is an arithmetic surface, and therefore is
X
itself. As a consequence, the morphisms
\eta_{D}
are injective, so that the following inequality holds:

h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,{\overline{L}}^{\otimes D})\leq h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\alpha^{*}{\overline{L}}^{\otimes D}).

As a consequence:

(8.2.11)

\limsup_{D\rightarrow+\infty}D^{-2}h^{0}_{\theta,J}(X,{\overline{L}}^{\otimes D})\leq\limsup_{D\rightarrow+\infty}D^{-2}h^{0}_{\theta,L^{2}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}},\mu;\alpha^{*}{\overline{L}}^{\otimes D}).

The inequality (
8.2.10
) now follows from the arithmetic Hilbert-Samuel formula
(
8.2.9
) in Theorem
8.2.5
and from the upper bound on the right-hand side of (
8.2.11
) provided by Theorem
8.2.2
.

(2) The Arakelov divisor
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is effective. Moreover, for every embedding
\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C},
the measure:

\omega(\alpha^{\rm an}_{\ast}g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})_{\mid X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C})}=\omega(\alpha_{\sigma\ast}g_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}})=\alpha_{\sigma\ast}\mu_{V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma}}

is positive.

To prove that
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is nef, it is enough to prove that the Arakelov degree along
\widehat{\alpha}(P)
of the Hermitian
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle attached to
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is nonnegative, since
\widehat{\alpha}(P)
is the unique component of the support of the cycle underlying
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})
. This follows from Lemma
7.2.7
, which shows that this Arakelov degree is
\geq e(\alpha)\,\widehat{\deg}\,P^{*}\overline{N}_{P,g}{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
, and therefore positive.

Corollary
7.2.9
shows that the self-intersection of
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathbb{C}}})
is positive, and therefore establishes that this Arakelov divsior is big.
∎

In this paragraph, we return to the notation of Theorem
8.1.2
and we derive the bound:

\deg f\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U)

from Proposition
8.2.6
.

To achieve this, we may replace
U
(resp.
V
) by the normalization of the closure of
U
(resp. of the graph of
f
) in some ambient projective spaces, and consequently assume that
U
and
V
are projective over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.

Let
{\overline{L}}
be a nef and big Hermitian line bundle on
X
. The pull-back
f^{*}{\overline{L}}
of
{\overline{L}}
on
Y
is nef and big, and we may apply Proposition
8.2.6
(1) to the morphism
\beta:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow Y
and to the Hermitian line bundle
f^{*}{\overline{L}}
on
Y
. This establishes the estimate:

(8.2.12)

f^{*}{\overline{L}}\cdot f^{*}{\overline{L}}\leq\frac{\big(\beta^{*}f^{*}{\overline{L}}\cdot(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})\big)^{2}}{\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}.

Using the equality
\alpha=f\circ\beta
and the projection formula of Proposition
7.2.2
, (
8.2.12
) becomes:

(8.2.13)

(\deg f)\;{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}\leq\frac{\big({\overline{L}}\cdot\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})\big)^{2}}{\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}.

By homogeneity, (
8.2.13
) holds when
{\overline{L}}
is an arbitrary nef and big
\mathbb{Q}
-Hermitian line bundle on
X
. In particular we may apply it to the
\mathbb{Q}
-Hermitian line bundle
\big(\mathcal{O}(\alpha(P)),\|.\|_{\alpha_{*}^{\mathrm{an}}g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}}\big)
associated to the Arakelov divisor
\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})
on
X
, which is nef and big by Proposition
8.2.6
(2). In this situation,

{\overline{L}}\cdot{\overline{L}}={\overline{L}}\cdot\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})=\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})\cdot\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}),

and (
8.2.13
) becomes:

\deg f\leq\frac{\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})\cdot\alpha_{*}(P,g_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}})}{\widehat{\deg}\,\overline{N}_{P{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}}}=:D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow U).

8.3.
The field of meromorphic functions on a pseudoconcave f.-a.​ arithmetic surface

8.3.1.
A finiteness theorem

A simple consequence of the algebraicity assertion in Theorem
8.2.1
and of the degree bound in Theorem
8.1.3
is the following finiteness property concerning the field of meromorphic functions on a pseudoconcave f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface.
6
6

6

Conversely the fact that the transcendence degree over
K
of this field is at most one is readily seen to imply Theorem
8.2.1
.

Theorem 8.3.1
.

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface. The following alternative holds: either
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})=K,
or
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is an extension of finite type and of transcendence degree one of
K
.

Moreover, for every integral, normal, projective arithmetic surface
X
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and every non-constant meromorphic map:

\alpha:=({\widehat{\alpha}},(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X

over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, then the degree of the field extension:

\alpha^{\ast}:K(X_{K}){\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}),\quad\varphi\longmapsto\big({\widehat{\alpha}}^{\ast}\varphi,(\alpha_{\sigma}^{\ast}\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big)

is finite and satisfies:

(8.3.1)

[{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})]\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X).

In the special case when
X=\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
the second part of Theorem
8.3.1
reads as follows:

Corollary 8.3.2
.

With the notation of Theorem
8.3.1
, for every
f\in{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\setminus K,

{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is a finite extension of the purely transcendental extension
K(f)
of
K
, and its degree satisfies the following upper bound:

(8.3.2)

[{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):K(f)]\leq D(f:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}).

Proof of Theorem
8.3.1
.

(1) We first prove the second part of the theorem. To achieve this, it is enough to show that, for any element
g
of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, the subfield
\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})(g)
of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is a finite extension of
\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})
and satisfies:

(8.3.3)

[\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})(g):\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})]\leq D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X).

To prove this, we identify
g
to a meromorphic map:

g:=\big(\widehat{g},(g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},

and we consider the meromorphic map defined as the product of
\alpha
and
g
:

\beta:=\big(({\widehat{\alpha}},\widehat{g}),(\alpha_{\sigma},g_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}\big):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow X\times_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}.

According to the algebraicity theorem
8.2.1
,
\beta
factors through a closed integral arithmetic surface
Y
in
X\times_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, and the projection :

{\rm pr}_{1}:Y\longrightarrow X

is dominant, hence surjective and generically finite, since
\alpha
is non-constant. By construction,
K(Y_{K})
may be identified, by means of
\beta^{\ast}
, with
\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})(g)
, which is therefore a finite extension of
\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})\simeq K(X_{K})
.

Moreover the meromorphic map
\beta
is readily seen to lift to the normalization
\nu:\widetilde{Y}\rightarrow Y
of
Y
. This defines a meromorphic map:

\widetilde{\beta}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow\widetilde{Y}

which fits into a commutative diagram:

(8.3.4)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.39583pt\raise-24.19446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise By construction, we have:

K(\widetilde{Y}_{K})=K(Y_{K})\simeq\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})(g),

and:

\deg{\rm pr}_{1}\circ\nu=[\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})(g):K(X_{K})].

Therefore the estimate (
8.3.3
) follows from Theorem
8.1.3
applied to the diagram (
8.3.4
).

(2) To complete the proof, observe that an element
f
of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\setminus K
is defined by an element
\widehat{f}_{K}
of
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}_{K})\simeq K((T))
not in
K
, and therefore is transcendental over
K
. As already observed in Corollary
8.3.2
, part (1) of this proof applied to
f
seen as a meromorphic map from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
establishes that
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is a finite extension of
K(f)
.
∎

Corollary 8.3.3
.

With the notation of Theorem
8.3.1
, if the map
{\widehat{\alpha}}
defines an isomorphism of formal schemes
7
7

7

We denote by
\widehat{X}_{Q}
the formal completion of
X
along
Q
.
:

{\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{X}_{Q},

where
Q:={\widehat{\alpha}}(P)
, and if the maps
(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
restrict to injections:

\alpha_{\sigma\mid\mathring{V}_{\sigma}}:\mathring{V}_{\sigma}\,{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\,X_{\sigma}(\mathbb{C}),

then
\alpha^{\ast}
establishes an isomorphism of fields:

\alpha^{\ast}:K(X_{K})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}).

A weaker result follows from
[
BCL09
, Theorem 7.9]
, which implies that
\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})
contains every element
f:=(\widehat{f},(f_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}})
of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
such that
\widehat{f}
belongs to
{\mathcal{O}}({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}})
.

Proof.

The assumptions on
\widehat{\alpha}
and
(\alpha_{\sigma})_{\sigma:K\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}
imply that
\alpha
is a morphism from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to
X
, that the ramification index
e(\alpha)
is 1, and that the overflow invariants
{\mathrm{Ex}}({\widehat{\alpha}}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)
and
{\mathrm{Ex}}(\alpha_{\sigma}:(V_{\sigma},P_{\sigma})
vanish. Consequently:

D(\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X)\leq 1,

and therefore (
8.3.1
) becomes:

[{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):\alpha^{\ast}K(X_{K})]=1.\qed

8.3.2.
Application: the arithmetic holonomicity theorem of
[
CDT21
]

Let us recall the statement of the arithmetic holonomicity theorem, which is one of the main ingredients of the proof of the unbounded denominators conjecture by Calegari-Dimitrov-Tang
[
CDT21
]
. We follow the notation of
[
CDT21
, section 2]
with only minor modifications.

Let
U
be a connected open subset of
\mathbb{C}
containing
0
, and let
x\in\mathbb{Q}[[T]]
be a formal power series such that:

(8.3.5)

x(0)=0\quad\mathrm{and}\quad x^{\prime}(0)=1.

We identify
x
with the morphism

x:\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{C}[[T]]\longrightarrow\widehat{U}_{0}

that it defines.

Consider the subset
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
of the space
\mathbb{Q}[[X]]
whose elements are those power series
f\in\mathbb{Q}[[X]]
satisfying the following two conditions:

(1)

the formal power series
f\circ x\in\mathbb{Q}[[T]]
lies in
\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
;

(2)

the formal power series
f
is holonomic on
U
, namely, there exists a nonzero linear differential operator
L
in
\mathbb{C}(X)[d/dx]
, without singularities on
U
, such that:

L(f)=0.

The set
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
is readily seen to be a
\mathbb{Z}
-subalgebra of
\mathbb{Q}[[X]]
.

We assume that the uniformization radius of the pointed Riemann surface
(U,0)
is strictly greater than
1
or, equivalently, that there exists a holomorphic map

\varphi:\overline{D(0,1)}^{+}\longrightarrow U

such that

\varphi(0)=0

and

(8.3.6)

|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|>1.

The following statement is
[
CDT21
, Theorem 2.0.2]
.

Theorem 8.3.4
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{Q}(X)\setminus\mathbb{Q}
be a nonconstant rational function without poles in
U
which defines an element of
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
– namely:

p\circ x\in\mathbb{Z}[[T]].

Then the
\mathbb{Q}(p)
-algebra
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[p]}\mathbb{Q}(p)
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbb{Q}(p)
-vector space. Moreover:

(8.3.7)

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}(p)}\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[p]}\mathbb{Q}(p)\leq e\frac{\int_{0}^{1}\log^{+}|p\circ\varphi(e^{2i\pi t})|dt}{\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|}.

Observe that the
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
does indeed contain the ring
\mathbb{Z}[p]
of polynomials in
p
, and therefore is a
\mathbb{Z}[p]
-algebra. Its “base change”
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}[p]}\mathbb{Q}(p)
is therefore a well-defined
\mathbb{Q}(p)
-algebra.

In the right-hand side of (
8.3.7
),
e
is the real number
\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}n!^{-1}
.

Keeping the same notation as above, assume for simplicity that
U
is invariant under complex conjugation and that
\varphi
is “real”, namely:

\varphi(\overline{z})=\overline{\varphi(z)}

for all
z\in\overline{D(0,1)}.

These conditions are actually satisfied in the application of Theorem
8.3.4
to the proof of the unbounded denominators conjecture, and will allow us to derive Theorem
8.3.4
from Corollary
8.3.2
with
K=\mathbb{Q}
applied to the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D}(0,1),\psi)
introduced in Chapter
6
.

When these conditions are not satisfied, Theorem
8.3.4
may be deduced from Corollary
8.3.2
with
K
an arbitrary imaginary quadratic field by a variant of the discussion below which we leave to the reader.

Consider the universal cover:

\nu:(\widetilde{U},O)\longrightarrow(U,0)

of the pointed Riemann surface
(U,0)
. The map
\nu
defines an isomorphism between the formal germs:

\widehat{\nu}:\widehat{\widetilde{U}}_{O}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{U}_{0}.

Moreover, the map
\varphi
factors through
\nu
; namely there exists a unique holomorphic map:

\widetilde{\varphi}:\overline{D(0,1)}^{+}\longrightarrow\widetilde{U}

such that:

\widetilde{\varphi}(0)=O\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nu\circ\widetilde{\varphi}=\varphi.

The formal germs at
0
of
\varphi
and
\widetilde{\varphi}
define isomorphisms of complex formal curves:

\widehat{\varphi}:\widehat{\mathbb{C}}_{0}=\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{C}[[z]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{U}_{0}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\widehat{\widetilde{\varphi}}:\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{C}[[z]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\widetilde{U}}_{O}.

The data of
U,x,\varphi,\nu,\widetilde{\varphi}
and their formal germs fit into the following commutative diagram of locally ringed spaces:

Recall that a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}=({\widehat{\mathcal{V}}},(V,P,i))

over
\mathbb{Z}
is defined by “gluing” the formal scheme
{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}
over
\mathbb{Z}
and the Riemann surface with boundary
V
according to the following diagram of locally ringed spaces:

\textstyle{\widehat{V}_{P}}
\textstyle{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathbb{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}.}

An element of the algebra

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to

, is defined by a pair of arrows

\textstyle{\widehat{V}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The diagram (
8.3.9
) defines the smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
\mathbb{Z}
:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x):={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\overline{D(0,1)},\widehat{\varphi}^{-1}\circ x)=(\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{Z}[[T]],(\overline{D(0,1)},\widehat{\varphi}^{-1}\circ x))

and the diagram (
8.3.10
) defines a smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
\mathbb{Z}
in a generalized sense
8
8

8

Indeed, the Riemann surface
\widetilde{U}
is not compact.
:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(U,x):=(\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{Z}[[T]],(\widetilde{U},\widehat{\nu}^{-1}\circ x)).

We may consider the
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras of regular functions on the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)
and
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(U,x)
:

\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)):=\{(\widehat{\alpha},\beta)\in\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\times\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{an}}(\overline{D(0,1)}^{+})\,|\,\widehat{\alpha}=\widehat{\beta}\circ\widehat{\varphi}^{-1}\circ x\}

and

\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(U,x)):=\{(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma)\in\mathbb{Z}[[T]]\times\mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{an}}(\widetilde{U})\,|\,\widehat{\alpha}=\widehat{\gamma}\circ\widehat{\nu}^{-1}\circ x\}

where
\widehat{\beta}
(resp.
\widehat{\gamma}
) denotes the Taylor expansion of the holomorphic function
\beta
(resp.
\gamma
) at the point
0
(resp.
O
) of
\mathbb{C}
(resp.
\widetilde{U}
).

We may define injective morphisms of
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras:

\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(U,x))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))

defined as follows:

(1)

If
f
is an element of
\mathbb{Q}[[X]]
that belongs to
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
, we let

i_{1}(f):=(f\circ x,\gamma)

where
\gamma
is the holomorphic function on the universal cover
\widetilde{U}
of
U
defined by the analytic continuation of the germ of the holomorphic function on
U
at
0
– or equivalently on
\widetilde{U}
at
0
– defined by
f
. The analyticity of
f
and the existence of its analytic continuation
\gamma
on
\widetilde{U}
follows from the holonomicity condition in the definition of
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
.

The condition
f\circ x=\widehat{\gamma}\circ\widehat{\nu}^{-1}
is clearly satisfied.

(2)

For every
(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma)
in
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(U,x))
, we let:

i_{2}(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma):=(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma\circ\widetilde{\varphi}).

Since
(\gamma\circ\widetilde{\varphi})^{\vee}\circ\widehat{\varphi}^{-1}=\widehat{\gamma}\circ\widehat{\nu}^{-1},

i_{2}(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma)
is indeed an element of
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))
.

Observe that the injective morphism

i_{2}\circ i_{1}:\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))

maps a series
f
in
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
to the pair
(\widehat{\alpha},\gamma)
in
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))
defined by
\widehat{\alpha}=f\circ x
, and by the analytic continuation
\gamma
of
f\circ\varphi
to
\overline{D(0,1)}^{+}
, which is a priori defined as the germ of a holomorphic function at the origin.

Furthermore, according to (
8.3.5
), the differential at
0
of
\widehat{\varphi}^{-1}\circ x
maps the generator
\partial/\partial T
of the normal bundle of
P:={\rm Spec\,}\mathbb{Z}
in
\mathrm{Spf}\,\mathbb{Z}[[T]]
to the vector
\varphi^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\partial/\partial z
in
T_{0}\overline{D(0,1)}^{+}
. Its capacitary norm is:

\|\varphi^{\prime}(0)^{-1}\partial/\partial z\|^{\mathrm{cap}}_{\overline{D(0,1)},0}=|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|^{-1}.

As a consequence, we have:

(8.3.11)

\widehat{\deg}\overline{N}_{P}{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)=\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|,

so that (
8.3.6
) — namely, the assumption
|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|>1
, itself a consequence of our assumption on the uniformization radius of
(U,0)
— is equivalent to the pseudoconcavity of the f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)
.

Applied to
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}={\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)
and to some
f
in
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))
, Corollary
8.3.2
takes the following form, when we take into account the expression (
8.1.9
) for the invariants
D\big(\alpha_{r}:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}_{r}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}}\big)
:

Corollary 8.3.5
.

Let
\alpha=(\widehat{\alpha},\beta)
be a non-constant element of
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)).
The field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))
is finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}(\alpha)
, and its degree satisfies:

[{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)):\mathbb{Q}(\alpha)]\leq({\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|})^{-1}{\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\log\big|\beta(e^{2i\pi t_{1}})-\beta(e^{2i\pi t_{2}})\big|\,dt_{1}dt_{2}}

Using the injective morphism of
\mathbb{Z}
-algebras:

i_{2}\circ i_{1}:\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z}){\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))

defined above, and the associated injective morphisms of fields:

\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\,{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\,\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x))\,{\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow}\,{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}(\varphi,x)),

this implies the following improved form of
[
CDT21
, Theorem 2.0.2]
:

Corollary 8.3.6
.

Let
f
be a non-constant element of
\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z}).
Then the
\mathbb{Q}(f)
-algebra

\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}[f]}\mathbb{Q}(f)

is finite-dimensional; in particular, it may be identified with the field
\mathrm{Frac}\,\mathcal{H}(U,x,\mathbb{Z})
. The dimension of this
\mathbb{Q}(\alpha)
-algebra is bounded above by:

(8.3.12)

\big({\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|}\big)^{-1}{\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\log\big|(f\circ\varphi)(e^{2i\pi t_{1}})-(f\circ\varphi)(e^{2i\pi t_{2}})\big|\,dt_{1}dt_{2}}.

As mentioned in the discussion of examples in Subsections
5.4.2
and
8.1.1
, the expression (
8.3.12
) admits the following upper bound in terms of the characteristic function
T_{f\circ\varphi}

à la
Nevanlinna-Ahlfors-Shimizu of
f\circ\varphi
:

\big({\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|}\big)^{-1}{\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\log\big|(f\circ\varphi)(e^{2i\pi t_{1}})-(f\circ\varphi)(e^{2i\pi t_{2}})\big|\,dt_{1}dt_{2}}\leq\big({\log|\varphi^{\prime}(0)|}\big)^{-1}\,T_{f\circ\varphi}(1).

Chapter 9
Pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces II: the algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
; fundamental groups of arithmetic surfaces

In this chapter, we establish some further properties of pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces and of their maps to quasi-projective arithmetic surfaces. These are obtained by combining the theorems in Chapter
8
with various classical techniques of arithmetic geometry.

In Section
9.1
, we establish some
arithmetic analogues of the finiteness results concerning
the universal meromorphic map
\varphi:{\mathcal{V}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
, attached to a germ of complex analytic surface
{\mathcal{V}}
fibered over a complex projective curve, and
the algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
introduced in Subsection
2.3.3
. Notably we show that the
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
attached to a pseudoconcave formal-analytic arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is finitely generated.

Section
9.2
is devoted to some arithmetic analogues of Lefschetz-Nori theorems on étale fundamental groups of complex algebraic surfaces presented in Part 1 as Proposition
1.3.2
and Theorem
2.2.4
.
In Section
9.3
, these arithmetic Lefschetz-Nori theorems are used to construct quasi-projective arithmetic surfaces that admit a finite étale fundamental group. Notably we show that this is the case of certain classical integral models of modular curves.

9.1.
A finiteness result for the algebra
\mathcal{O}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and a structure theorem for morphisms to affine arithmetic surfaces

9.1.1.
Universal morphisms to affine arithmetic schemes and finiteness

The universal meromorphic map
\varphi:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}

Let
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
be a pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
such that the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
of meromorphic functions over
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
is not reduced to
K
.

According to Theorem
8.3.1
, the field
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is a finitely generated field extension of
K
of transcendence degree one. Moreover if the divisor of some element
f\in{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
does not contain
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|={\rm im\,}P
with a negative multiplicity, then the restriction of
f
to
|{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}|
, or equivalently its pull-back by
P
, defines an element of
K
. The partially defined map:

(9.1.1)

{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\dashrightarrow K,\quad f\longmapsto P^{\ast}f

defines a place of
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
over
K
.

As a consequence, there exist a smooth projective geometrically irreducible curve
C
over
K
, endowed with a
K
-rational point
O
, and an isomorphism of
K
-algebras:

\iota:K(C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})

whose composition with (
9.1.1
) is the place of
K(C)
over
K
defined by
O
. Moreover the pair
(C,\iota)
is unique up to unique isomorphism, and the point
O
in
C(K)
is uniquely determined by this condition.

We may consider a projective normal arithmetic surface:

(9.1.2)

{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}

which is a model of
C
— so that the
K
-scheme
{\mathcal{C}}_{K}
is isomorphic to
C
— and the section
O
of (
9.1.2
) that extends the rational point
O
in
C(K)\simeq{\mathcal{C}}(K)
.

The isomorphism
\iota
defines a canonical meromorphic map:

\varphi:=(\widehat{\varphi},(\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}}):{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}

such that the composition
\widehat{\varphi}\circ P
coincides with
O
.

The map
\varphi
is easily checked to be a “universal meromorphic map” from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to a projective
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-scheme and to play the role of the universal meromorphic map
\varphi:{\mathcal{V}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{alg}}
studied in Subsection
2.3.3
in the geometric case.

The construction in Subsection
2.3.4
, suitably adapted to the arithmetic setting, is expected to produce examples of pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surfaces
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\neq K
for which the map
\varphi
may be ramified along
P
. We leave the details to the interested reader.

The main finiteness result

Observe that the fraction field
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
of the domain
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
embeds in
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, and, like
{\mathcal{M}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
, is an extension of
K
of finite type, of transcendence degree at most one. According to Theorem
8.3.1
, if
\varphi
is an element of
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\setminus{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, then the field
\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is an algebraic extension of
K(\varphi)
, and its degree satisfies the upper bound:

(9.1.3)

[\mathrm{Frac}\,{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}):K(\varphi)]\leq D(\varphi:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}}).

Note that the inequality above is enough in itself to prove the results of
[
CDT21
]
.

In Subsection
2.3.3
, we have introduced the
\mathbb{C}[\mathring{C}]
-algebra
{\mathcal{A}}
, which is a geometric counterpart of the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
and we have shown that, in the pseudoconcave case, it is a finitely generated
\mathbb{C}[\mathring{C}]
-algebra, as a consequence of a finiteness result of Zariski
[
Zar54
]
concerning the algebra of regular functions on quasi-projective varieties over a field.

In this section, we establish an arithmetic analogue of this finiteness result.

Theorem 9.1.1
.

For every pseudoconcave smooth f.-a.​​ arithmetic surface
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
, the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-algebra
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
is finitely generated.

The universal map to an affine arithmetic surface

According to Theorem
9.1.1
, the scheme:

{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}}:={\rm Spec\,}{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})

is an affine scheme of finite type over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.
When
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\neq{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}},
it is an integral normal affine arithmetic surface over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
.
Moreover the identification

{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})\simeq\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}}})

defines a tautological morphism over
{\rm Spec\,}{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}},

and this morphism
\alpha
is easily seen to be universal among the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-morphisms from
{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}
to affine arithmetic
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes.

We will actually prove Theorem
9.1.1
by first constructing the universal morphism
\alpha:{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\longrightarrow{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}}
to affine arithmetic
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-schemes – this is the content of Theorem
9.1.10
– and then using it to prove the relevant finiteness result on
{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}})
by proving that
\alpha
induces an isomorphism

\Gamma({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}^{\mathrm{aff}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{O}}({\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}).

9.1.2.
Preliminary results

We gather a few general results of independent interest.

Monotonicity and discreteness properties of non-Archimedean overflows

Proposition 9.1.2
.

Consider a commutative diagram of morphisms over
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
:

\textstyle{S_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2}}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

where
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are integral, normal, affine arithmetic surfaces and
p
is birational. Then

(9.1.4)

\mathrm{Ex}(\widehat{\gamma}_{1}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X_{1})\geq\mathrm{Ex}(\widehat{\gamma}_{2}:{\widehat{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow X_{2}),

and equality holds in (
9.1.4
) if and only if there exists a neighborhood
U
of the
{{\mathcal{O}}_{K}}
-point
\gamma_{1}(P)
in
X_{1}
such that

p_{|p^{-1}(U)}:p^{-1}(U)\rightarrow U

is an isomorphism. Furthermore, if equality does not hold in (
9.1.4
) and
\widehat{\gamma}_{2}
is quasi-finite, then:

(9.1.5)
